Third Order Upper Bound for the Ground State Energy of the Dilute Bose Gas
Morris Brooks, Jakob Oldenburg, Diane Saint Aubin, Benjamin Schlein

Abstract
We consider a dilute Bose gas in the thermodynamic limit. We prove an upper bound for the ground state energy per unit volume, capturing the expected third order term, as predicted by Wu, Hugenholtz-Pines and Sawada.



1
Introduction

We consider a dilute Bose gas, consisting of
N
particles moving in a large, thermodynamic box
\Lambda_{t}
with Dirichlet boundary conditions and interacting through a two-body potential
V
. The Hamilton operator of the system is given by

H_{L}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}-\Delta_{i}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq N}V(x_{i}-x_{j})

(1)

and acts on
L^{2}_{s}(\Lambda_{t}^{N})
, the subspace of
L^{2}(\Lambda_{t}^{N})
consisting of all functions that are symmetric with respect to permutations of the
N
particles.

We are going to assume that
V\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
is radial, stable (see
[
34
, Section 3.5]
), with compact support, with no two-body bound states, and with positive scattering length
\mathfrak{a}>0
as defined in
[
29
, Appendix C]
. In particular, we can consider any non-negative, radial and compactly supported
V\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
.
Recall that the scattering length of
V
can be recovered from the solution of the zero-energy scattering equation

\left[-\Delta+\frac{1}{2}V\right]f=0

(2)

with the boundary condition
f(x)\to 1
as
|x|\to\infty
, since
f(x)=1-\mathfrak{a}/|x|
, outside the support of
V
. Equivalently,

8\pi\mathfrak{a}=\int V(x)f(x)dx\,.

Notice that
f\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, as shown in
[
26
, Theorem 11.7]
.

We denote by
E(N,\Lambda_{t})
the ground state energy of the gas, ie. the lowest eigenvalue of the operator (
1
). We consider the thermodynamic limit
N,|\Lambda_{t}|\to\infty
, at fixed density
\rho=N/|\Lambda_{t}|
. We are interested in the energy per unit volume

e(\rho)=\lim_{N,|\Lambda_{t}|\to\infty,\rho=N/|\Lambda_{t}|}\frac{E(N,\Lambda_{t})}{|\Lambda_{t}|}

in the dilute limit
\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3}\to 0
. The following theorem is the main result of our paper.

Theorem 1
.

Let
V\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
be radial, stable and with compact support. Assume that
V
does not admit two-body bound states and let
\mathfrak{a}>0
be its scattering length. Then, there is a constant
C>0
such that

e(\rho)\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}\left(1+\frac{128}{15\sqrt{\pi}}\sqrt{\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3}}+8\left(\frac{4\pi}{3}-\sqrt{3}\right)\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3}\log(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})+C\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3}\right)

(3)

for all
\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3}>0
small enough.

The bound (
3
) is expected to capture the correct ground state energy per unit volume, up to corrections of order
\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})
, in the dilute limit
\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3}\to 0
, that are expected to no longer depend universally on
\mathfrak{a}
. The expression on the r.h.s. of (
3
) has a long history in the physics literature. The first two terms in the asymptotic expansion have been predicted in 1957, by Lee-Huang-Yang
[
25
]
, based on previous work of Bogoliubov
[
8
]
. Two years later, in 1959, Wu
[
38
]
and then also Hugenholtz-Pines
[
24
]
and Sawada
[
35
]
predicted the third logarithmic correction. At the level of rigorous mathematical derivation, the leading order term has been established by Dyson
[
16
]
as an upper bound and, 40 years later, by Lieb-Yngvason
[
31
]
as a lower bound. For potentials with negative part, the corresponding result was obtained in
[
37
]
. An upper bounds including the Lee-Huang-Yang term has been proven in
[
36
]
, for sufficiently regular interactions (previously, an upper bound reaching the Lee-Huang-Yang order, but only recovering the correct constant in the limit of weak coupling, was shown in
[
17
]
). More recently, a simpler upper bound has been derived in
[
4
]
, for a larger class of interactions. The case of hard-sphere potential, however, remains open (for hard-spheres, the currently best available upper bound on the ground state energy per unit volume has been shown in
[
2
]
, with an error of the Lee-Huang-Yang order). A lower bound resolving the Lee-Huang-Yang contribution has been first shown in
[
18
]
and, for more general interactions including hard spheres, in
[
19
]
. Recently, a new proof of the lower bound, applying also to the free energy at low positive temperature, has been obtained in
[
20
]
(a matching low temperature upper bound has been derived in
[
21
]
). The result of
[
20
]
has been extended to potentials including hard spheres in
[
22
]
.

Theorem
1
establishes an upper bound on the ground state energy per unit volume of a dilute Bose gas, interacting through
V\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, consistent with Wu’s prediction. A complete proof of the validity of Wu’s formula in the thermodynamic limit requires a matching lower bound, which remains a challenging open problem. Remark, however, that a rigorous derivation of Wu’s correction is available in the Gross-Pitaevskii regime, where
N
particles are confined in the unit torus and interact through a potential with scattering length of the order
1/N
. In this limit, the ground state energy and the low-energy excitation spectrum have been resolved to the Lee-Huang-Yang order in
[
5
,
6
]
, through a rigorous version of Bogoliubov theory (leading order estimates and a proof of Bose-Einstein condensation have been previously obtained in
[
30
,
27
,
28
]
) . Recently, extending the approach of
[
5
,
6
]
, Wu’s logarithmic correction to the ground state energy has been resolved in
[
9
]
; we will use ideas from
[
9
]
also in the current paper, in particular in Section
2.2
to define the kernel of the cubic transformation. Related results in the Gross-Pitaevskii regime (and beyond) have been derived in
[
1
,
3
,
7
,
10
,
11
,
12
,
13
,
14
,
15
,
23
,
32
,
33
]
.

The proof of Theorem
1
is based on a well-known localization technique; given a grand canonical trial state
\psi_{L}
on a periodic box of size
L=\rho^{-\gamma}
, with approximately
\rho L^{3}
particles and with approximately the correct energy per unit volume, we can construct a grand canonical trial state on the thermodynamic box
\Lambda_{t}
with approximately the correct energy, first extending
\psi_{L}
to a trial state
\psi_{L}^{\text{dir}}
on a Dirichlet box of size slightly larger than
L
and then combining several copies of
\psi_{L}^{\text{dir}}
to cover the whole volume of
\Lambda_{t}
(to avoid interactions among particles in different boxes, they must be separated by small corridors, broader than the support of
V
). At the end, (
3
) follows by the equivalence of ensembles.

The main part of the paper is devoted to the construction of the grand canonical trial state on the periodic box
\Lambda_{L}
, of size
L=\rho^{-\gamma}
for appropriate
\gamma>0
(to control the localization error produced by imposing Dirichlet boundary conditions, we will need
\gamma\geq 3/2
). In fact, we find it more convenient to rescale lengths, and work on the unit torus
\Lambda\equiv\Lambda_{1}\simeq[-1/2;1/2]^{3}
. Denoting by
N=\rho L^{3}=\rho^{1-3\gamma}
the expected number of particles in
\Lambda_{L}
, we find
\rho=N^{-1/(3\gamma-1)}
and
L=N^{\gamma/(3\gamma-1)}=N^{1-\kappa}
, where we introduced the parameter
\kappa=(2\gamma-1)/(3\gamma-1)
. For
\kappa\in[0;2/3)
, we consider the Hamilton operator

\mathcal{H}_{N}=\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}p^{2}a_{p}^{*}a_{p}+\frac{N^{\kappa}}{2N}\sum_{p,q,r\in\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(r/N^{1-\kappa})a_{p+r}^{*}a_{q-r}^{*}a_{q}a_{p}

(4)

acting on the bosonic Fock space
\mathcal{F}(\Lambda)=\oplus_{n\geq 0}L^{2}_{s}(\Lambda^{n})
. Here,
\Lambda^{*}=2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{3}
is the dual lattice and, for
p\in\Lambda^{*}
,
a_{p}^{*},a_{p}
are the usual creation and annihilation operators, satisfying canonical commutation relations. Moreover, we slightly abuse notation and denote
V
and its periodic extension in the same way.

Theorem 2
.

Under the same assumptions on
V
as in Theorem
1
, we have that for any
\kappa\in[1/2,2/3)
and
N
large enough there exists
\Psi_{N}\in\mathcal{F}(\Lambda)
,
\lVert\Psi_{N}\rVert=1
such that

\langle\Psi_{N},\mathcal{N}\Psi_{N}\rangle\geq N,\quad\langle\Psi_{N},\mathcal{N}^{2}\Psi_{N}\rangle\leq CN^{2}

(5)

and

\begin{split}\langle\Psi_{N},\mathcal{H}_{N}\Psi_{N}\rangle\leq\;&4\pi\mathfrak{a}N^{1+\kappa}+\frac{512\sqrt{\pi}}{15}\mathfrak{a}^{5/2}N^{5\kappa/2}\\
&+32\pi\left(\frac{4\pi}{3}-\sqrt{3}\right)\mathfrak{a}^{4}N^{4\kappa-1}\log(N^{3\kappa-2})+CN^{4\kappa-1}.\end{split}

(6)

Remark.
The restriction to
\kappa\geq 1/2
makes sure that finite size errors of order
N^{2\kappa}
are negligible.

With Theorem
2
, we can prove our main result.

Proof of Theorem
1
.

To translate the upper bound (
6
) into a bound for the ground state energy per unit volume in the thermodynamic limit we use
[
4
, Prop. 1.2]
, which states that, if
R<\ell<L
, with
R
the radius of the support of the potential
V
, and if
\psi_{L}\in\mathcal{F}(\Lambda_{L})
is normalized and such that

\langle\psi_{L},\mathcal{N}\psi_{L}\rangle\geq\rho(1+c\rho)(L+2\ell+R)^{3},\qquad\langle\psi_{L},\mathcal{N}^{2}\psi_{L}\rangle\leq C\rho^{2}(L+2\ell+R)^{6}

(7)

then we have

e(\rho)\leq\frac{\langle\psi_{L},\mathcal{H}\psi_{L}\rangle}{L^{3}}+\frac{C}{L^{4}\ell}\langle\psi_{L},\mathcal{N}\psi_{L}\rangle

(8)

with the Hamilton operator

\mathcal{H}=\sum_{p\in\Lambda_{L}^{*}}p^{2}a_{p}^{*}a_{p}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{p,q,r\in\Lambda^{*}_{L}}\hat{V}(r)a_{p+r}^{*}a_{q-r}^{*}a_{q}a_{p}

acting on
\mathcal{F}(\Lambda_{L})
(where
\Lambda^{*}_{L}=2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{3}/L
).

Notice that the state
\psi_{L}\in\mathcal{F}(\Lambda_{L})
, whose energy appears on the r.h.s. of (
8
) and provides an upper bound to
e(\rho)
, must have, effectively, a slightly larger density than
\rho
(to compensate the fact that, to construct the thermodynamic trial state, we first need to increase a bit the size of the box
\Lambda_{L}
to impose Dirichlet boundary conditions and to make sure that particles in different boxes do not interact).

Given a density
\rho>0
, we thus choose
\widetilde{\rho}>\rho
(to be specified later on) and we apply Theorem
2
. After rescaling lengths we find, for any
\gamma>1
(reflecting the condition
\kappa=(2\gamma-1)/(3\gamma-1)\in(1/2;2/3)
), a normalized
\psi_{L}\in\mathcal{F}(\Lambda_{L})
on the periodic box of size
L=\widetilde{\rho}^{-\gamma}
(so that
N=\widetilde{\rho}^{1-3\gamma}
), with

\langle\psi_{L},\mathcal{N}\psi_{L}\rangle\geq\widetilde{\rho}L^{3},\qquad\langle\psi_{L},\mathcal{N}^{2}\psi_{L}\rangle\leq C\widetilde{\rho}^{2}L^{6}

and (since
\mathcal{H}_{N}
is unitarily equivalent to
L^{2}\mathcal{H}
) with

\frac{\langle\psi_{L},\mathcal{H}\psi_{L}\rangle}{L^{3}}\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\widetilde{\rho}^{2}\left(1+\frac{128}{15\sqrt{\pi}}\sqrt{\widetilde{\rho}\mathfrak{a}^{3}}+8\left(\frac{4\pi}{3}-\sqrt{3}\right)\widetilde{\rho}\mathfrak{a}^{3}\log(\widetilde{\rho}\mathfrak{a}^{3})+C\widetilde{\rho}\mathfrak{a}^{3}\right).

To make sure that (
7
) holds true, we set
\ell=L^{\alpha}
, for
0<\alpha<1
, and we apply the implicit function theorem to find
\widetilde{\rho}>\rho
such that

\widetilde{\rho}=\rho(1+c\rho)(1+2\widetilde{\rho}^{\gamma(1-\alpha)}+R\widetilde{\rho}^{\gamma})^{3}.

From (
8
), we conclude therefore that

e(\rho)\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\widetilde{\rho}^{2}\left(1+\frac{128}{15\sqrt{\pi}}\sqrt{\widetilde{\rho}\mathfrak{a}^{3}}+8\left(\frac{4\pi}{3}-\sqrt{3}\right)\widetilde{\rho}\mathfrak{a}^{3}\log(\widetilde{\rho}\mathfrak{a}^{3})+C\widetilde{\rho}\mathfrak{a}^{3}\right)+C\widetilde{\rho}^{1+\gamma(1+\alpha)}.

Since
\widetilde{\rho}^{2}\leq\rho^{2}(1+C\rho+C\rho^{\gamma(1-\alpha)})
, we obtain

\begin{split}e(\rho)\leq\;&4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}\left(1+\frac{128}{15\sqrt{\pi}}\sqrt{\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3}}+8\left(\frac{4\pi}{3}-\sqrt{3}\right)\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3}\log(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})\right)\\
&+C(\rho^{3}+\rho^{2+\gamma(1-\alpha)}+\rho^{1+\gamma(1+\alpha)}).\end{split}

Choosing
\alpha=1/(2\gamma)
, we find the error
\rho^{3/2+\gamma}
. Choosing
\gamma\geq 3/2
(which corresponds to
\kappa\geq 4/7
, using the language of Theorem
2
), we arrive at the desired bound.
∎

The rest of the paper is devoted to the proof of Theorem
2
. Let us briefly explain, on a heuristic level, the main ideas involved in the proof of (
3
). We start from Bogoliubov theory
[
8
]
. First, we factor out the Bose-Einstein condensate, conjugating the Hamiltonian (
4
) with a Weyl operator generating a coherent state with, in average,
N_{0}
particles with momentum
p=0
.
In the second step, we conjugate the resulting excitation Hamiltonian
with a Bogoliubov transformation
e^{B^{*}-B}
, with

B=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{|p|\geq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}}\mu_{p}a_{p}a_{-p}

(9)

and with coefficients

\mu_{p}=-\frac{1}{4}\log\Big(1+\frac{2N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})}{p^{2}}\Big)

satisfying

\tanh(2\mu_{p})=-\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})/(p^{2}+\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})).

(10)

This leads us to a new, unitarily equivalent, Hamiltonian

\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\simeq C_{N}+\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{C}_{N}+\mathcal{C}_{N}^{*}+\mathcal{V}_{N}

(11)

where the constant
C_{N}
gives the correct ground state energy to leading order (and already contains some terms contributing to the Lee-Huang-Yang correction),
\mathcal{K}
is the kinetic energy operator,

\mathcal{C}^{*}_{N}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{p,r\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\hat{V}(r/N^{1-\kappa})\sigma_{p}a_{p+r}^{*}a_{-r}^{*}a_{p}^{*}

with
\sigma_{p}=\sinh\mu_{p}
and

\mathcal{V}_{N}=\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{r,p,q\in\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(r/N^{1-\kappa})a_{p+r}^{*}a_{q}^{*}a_{q+r}a_{p}\,.

The two Bogoliubov transformations that have been used, for example, in
[
5
,
6
]
, are combined here into (
9
). For large momenta
|p|\gg N^{\kappa/2}
,
\mu_{p}\simeq-N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})/2p^{2}
approximates the solution of the zero-energy scattering equation (
2
) and the action of (
9
) helps renormalizing the interaction potential. For
|p|\lesssim N^{\kappa/2}
, on the other hand, the Bogoliubov transformation diagonalizes the quadratic terms in the excitation Hamiltonian (the identity (
10
) makes sure that
e^{B^{*}-B}
diagonalizes exactly a quadratic Hamiltonian with potential
Vf
). In (
9
), we restrict the sum to momenta
|p|\geq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}
, with the exponent
\beta=2-3\kappa
; contributions arising from
|p|<N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}
do not change the energy, up to order
N^{4\kappa-1}
(to resolve the Lee-Huang-Yang energy, it is enough to consider
|p|\gtrsim N^{\kappa/2}
).

Wu
[
38
]
proposed to use second order perturbation theory to obtain a better approximation for the ground state energy, essentially treating
\mathcal{K}
as the unperturbed Hamiltonian and
\mathcal{C}_{N}+\mathcal{C}_{N}^{*}+\mathcal{V}_{N}
as the perturbations (first order perturbation theory gives a vanishing contribution, because the ground state of
\mathcal{K}
is the vacuum and the vacuum expectation of
\mathcal{C}_{N},\mathcal{C}_{N}^{*},\mathcal{V}_{N}
is zero). This remark suggests that a good trial state for the renormalized Hamiltonian
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}
could have the form

\begin{split}\xi&=\Omega-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}\mathcal{K}}\sum_{|r|\geq N^{\kappa/2},|v|\geq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}}\widehat{Vf}(r/N^{1-\kappa})\sigma_{v}a_{r+v}^{*}a_{-r}^{*}a_{-v}^{*}\Omega\\
&=\Omega-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{|r|\geq N^{\kappa/2},|v|\geq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}}\frac{\widehat{Vf}(r/N^{1-\kappa})\sigma_{v}}{(r+v)^{2}+r^{2}+v^{2}}a_{r+v}^{*}a_{-r}^{*}a_{-v}^{*}\Omega=:\Omega+A^{*}\Omega\end{split}

(12)

where we used the renormalized potential to make sure that the energy of
\xi
only depends on the scattering length and where we restricted the sum to
|r|\geq N^{\kappa/2}
and
|v|\geq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}
, since contributions from smaller momenta do not affect the energy, to order
N^{4\kappa-1}
. It turns out that, with this choice of
\xi
,

\langle\xi,(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{C}_{N}+\mathcal{C}_{N}^{*}+\mathcal{V}_{N})\xi\rangle=\langle\Omega,(A\mathcal{C}_{N}^{*}+\mathcal{C}_{N}A^{*})\Omega\rangle+\langle\Omega,A(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N})A^{*}\Omega\rangle

(13)

leads, together with the constant term
C_{N}
in (
11
), exactly to the correct expression for the ground state energy, including the third order term in (
3
). The problem with this approach is the fact that
\xi
is not normalized. Since

\|\xi\|^{2}=1+\|A^{*}\Omega\|^{2}\simeq CN^{3\kappa-1}

the trial state
\xi/\|\xi\|
only provides a good upper bound for the ground state energy, if
\kappa=0
. To avoid problems with the normalization, we will consider a trial state defined through a unitary map. We define, formally,
\xi=e^{A^{*}-A}\Omega
, with
A
as in (
12
). Since

\langle\Omega,[\mathcal{C}_{N}+\mathcal{C}_{N}^{*},A^{*}-A]\Omega\rangle=\langle\Omega,(\mathcal{C}_{N}A^{*}+A\mathcal{C}_{N}^{*})\Omega\rangle

and

\langle\Omega,[[\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N},A^{*}-A],A^{*}-A]\Omega\rangle=2\langle\Omega,A(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V})A^{*}\Omega\rangle

the trial state
\xi
does indeed generate the contributions (
13
), leading to Wu’s correction. To obtain a rigorous upper bound, though, we would have to show that
e^{A^{*}-A}
is well-defined as a unitary operator and we would have to show convergence of its Duhamel expansion.

On the one hand, we observe that the vacuum expectation of multiple commutators like
[A,[A^{*},[A,\dots[A^{*},O]\dots]
gets smaller, with increasing number of commutators. In fact, for every two additional commutators, we have four more momenta to sum up. When considering the vacuum expectation, however, we also have three more delta functions. Effectively, every new pair of commutators with
A,A^{*}
produces therefore only one additional momentum sum, typically of the order
\|\sigma\|_{2}^{2}\simeq N^{3\kappa/2}
. Since each factor
A,A^{*}
carries a small constant
N^{-1/2}
, every two new commutators generate an additional small factor
N^{3\kappa/2-1}\ll 1
.

On the other hand, to
control error terms arising in the Duhamel expansion, it is very useful to introduce a cutoff on the local number of particles. For
x\in\Lambda
and
\ell>0
, we consider the operator

\mathcal{N}_{\ell}(x)=\int_{|x-y|\leq\ell}dy\,a_{y}^{*}a_{y}

measuring the number of particles in a ball of radius
\ell
around
x
. Since we used an infrared cutoff in momentum space (in fact, we will work with smooth cutoffs), the kernel
\check{\sigma}(x)
exhibits fast decay, for
|x|\gg\ell_{\sigma}:=N^{-\kappa/2+\beta/4}
. Similarly, the kernel
\check{A}(y,x)
associated with the cubic operator
A
defined in (
12
), is very small for
|x|\gg\ell_{\sigma}
or for
|y|\gg\ell_{\eta}:=N^{-\kappa/2}
. In fact,
\check{\sigma}(x)
and
\check{A}(y,x)
also decay for
|x|<\ell_{\sigma}
, with rate proportional to
|x|^{-5/2}
. This implies that their
L^{2}
-norm is essentially supported in the region
|x|\leq\ell_{B}=N^{-\kappa/2+\beta/12}\ll\ell_{\sigma}
, in the sense that, if we introduce a lattice
\Lambda_{B}=\ell_{B}\mathbb{Z}^{3}
and, for every
u\in\Lambda_{B}\cap\Lambda
, we define
\sigma_{u}=\sigma\mathbbm{1}_{B_{u}}
, with
B_{u}
the box with side length
\ell_{B}
centered at
u
, then

\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{B}}\|\check{\sigma}_{u}\|_{2}\leq CN^{3\kappa/4+\epsilon}

(14)

for any
\epsilon>0
. Up to the additional factor
N^{\epsilon}
, this is comparable with
\|\check{\sigma}\|_{2}
. To take advantage from the decay of the kernels
\check{\sigma}
,
\check{A}
, we will therefore modify the trial state, defining
\xi=e^{A^{*}\Theta-\Theta A}\Omega
, where we introduced an operator
\Theta
, making sure that

\Theta\mathcal{N}_{\ell_{B}}(x)\Theta\leq N^{\epsilon}

(15)

for any
\epsilon>0
and every
x\in\Lambda
, cutting off the local number of particles (more precisely, the local number of cubic excitations) in balls of radius
\ell_{B}=N^{-\kappa/2+\beta/12}
. Since the expected (by Duhamel expansion) average number of particles generated by the cubic transformation in a ball of radius
\ell_{B}
is of the order

\ell^{3}_{B}\|A^{*}\Omega\|^{2}\simeq\ell^{3}_{B}N^{3\kappa-1}\simeq N^{-\beta/4}\ll 1,

the cutoff does not substantially change the energy of the trial state (but it is nevertheless important to control error terms).

To explain how we use the cutoff
\Theta
, let us try to estimate the number of particles generated by the unitary operator
e^{\Theta\widetilde{A}^{*}-\widetilde{A}\Theta}
, where we replaced
A
by its simplified version

\widetilde{A}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{|p|\geq N^{\kappa/2},|q|\geq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}}\eta_{p}\sigma_{q}a_{p+q}a_{-p}a_{-q}

with
\eta_{p}=-N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})/2p^{2}
. We have

\langle e^{\Theta\widetilde{A}^{*}-\widetilde{A}\Theta}\Omega,\mathcal{N}e^{\Theta\widetilde{A}^{*}-\widetilde{A}\Theta}\Omega\rangle=2\text{Re }\int_{0}^{1}ds\langle e^{s(\Theta\widetilde{A}^{*}-\widetilde{A}\Theta)}\Omega,[\mathcal{N},\widetilde{A}]\Theta e^{s(\Theta\widetilde{A}^{*}-\widetilde{A}\Theta)}\Omega\rangle.

(16)

Setting
\xi_{s}=e^{s(\Theta\widetilde{A}^{*}-\widetilde{A}\Theta)}\Omega
and switching to position space, we find

\begin{split}\big|\langle\xi_{s},[\mathcal{N},\widetilde{A}]\Theta\xi_{s}\rangle\big|&=3\int dxdy\,|\check{\eta}(x-y)||\check{\sigma}(x-z)|\|a_{x}a_{y}a_{z}\Theta\xi_{s}\|\|\xi_{s}\|\\
&\leq 3\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{B}}\int dxdy\,|\check{\eta}(x-y)||\check{\sigma}_{u}(x-z)|\|a_{x}a_{y}a_{z}\Theta\xi_{s}\|\|\xi_{s}\|.\end{split}

By Cauchy-Schwarz and using that
\check{\sigma}_{u}(x-z)
is supported on
|x-z-u|\leq\ell_{B}
and
\check{\eta}(x-y)
is essentially supported on
|x-y|\leq\ell_{\eta}
, we conclude that

\begin{split}\big|\langle\xi_{s},[\mathcal{N},\widetilde{A}]\Theta\xi_{s}\rangle\big|&\leq 3\|\check{\eta}\|_{2}\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{B}}\|\check{\eta}\|_{2}\|\check{\sigma}_{u}\|_{2}\Big(\int dx\,\|\mathcal{N}^{1/2}_{\ell_{B}}(x-u)\mathcal{N}^{1/2}_{\ell_{\eta}}(x)a_{x}\Theta\xi_{s}\|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}.\end{split}

(17)

With
\|\eta\|_{2}\leq CN^{3\kappa/4}
and using (
14
) and (
15
) (after passing
\Theta
through
a_{x}
), we obtain

\big|\langle\xi_{s},[\mathcal{N},\widetilde{A}]\Theta\xi_{s}\rangle\big|\leq CN^{3\kappa/2-1/2+\epsilon}\|\mathcal{N}^{1/2}\xi_{s}\|\|\xi_{s}\|\leq CN^{3\kappa-1+\epsilon}\|\xi_{s}\|^{2}+C\|\mathcal{N}^{1/2}\xi_{s}\|^{2}.

Inserting in (
16
) and applying Gronwall’s Lemma, we arrive at

\langle e^{\widetilde{A}^{*}\Theta-\Theta\widetilde{A}}\Omega,\mathcal{N}e^{\widetilde{A}^{*}\Theta-\Theta\widetilde{A}}\Omega\rangle\leq CN^{3\kappa-1+\epsilon}

which is almost the optimal result (one can remove the additional
N^{\epsilon}
, expanding (
16
) to the next order; this will be shown for the full cubic operator
A
in Section
3.2
). Deriving such an estimate bounding the r.h.s. of (
17
) with the global, rather than the local, number of particles operator would have been impossible. Also, localizing
\sigma
on boxes of size
\ell_{\sigma}
, rather than
\ell_{B}\ll\ell_{\sigma}
, would have led to a worse, non-optimal, dependence on
N
.

The plan of the paper is as follows. In the next section, we define our trial state
\psi_{N}=W_{N_{0}}e^{B^{*}-B}e^{A^{*}\Theta-\Theta A}\Omega
, where
W_{N_{0}}
is the Weyl operator, generating the condensate in the zero momentum state,
e^{B^{*}-B}
is the Bogoliubov transformation and
e^{A^{*}\Theta-\Theta A}
is the cubic transformation. To this end, in Subsections
2.1
,
2.2
we define the coefficients of
B
and
A
and we study their properties, in momentum and in position space. In Subsection
2.3
, we introduce the cutoff
\Theta
for the local number of particles operator. In Section
3
, we will then compute the energy of our trial state; in subsection
3.1
we first compute the action of the Bogoliubov transformation on the Hamilton operator. This reduces the problem to the computation of certain expectations, in the state
\xi=e^{A^{*}\Theta-\Theta A}\Omega
, which will be discussed in Subsections
3.2
,
3.3
. This will allow us to complete the proof of Theorem
2
in Subsection
3.4
.

Acknowledgements.
We gratefully acknowledge financial support from the Swiss National Science Foundation through the Grant “Dynamical and energetic properties of Bose-Einstein condensates” and from the European Research Council through the ERC-AdG CLaQS.

2
The trial state

This section is devoted to the construction of the trial state
\psi_{N}\in\mathcal{F}(\Lambda)
, which will be later shown to satisfy (
5
) and (
6
).

We will consider a trial state of the form

\psi_{N}=W_{N_{0}}e^{B-B^{*}}e^{A^{*}\Theta-\Theta A}\Omega.

(18)

Here,
\Omega\in\mathcal{F}(\Lambda)
is the vacuum vector. Moreover,

W_{N_{0}}=\exp(\sqrt{N_{0}}a_{0}^{*}-\text{h.c.})

(19)

is a Weyl operator, satisfying

W_{N_{0}}^{*}a_{p}W_{N_{0}}=a_{p}+\delta_{p,0}\sqrt{N_{0}}

(20)

for all
p\in\Lambda^{*}=2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{3}
and generating a coherent state with (in average)
N_{0}
particles with momentum
p=0
(the Bose-Einstein condensate).

On the other hand,
e^{B-B^{*}}
and
e^{A^{*}\Theta-\Theta A}
are unitary maps, with

B=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}\backslash\{0\}}\mu_{p}a_{p}a_{-p}

(21)

quadratic and

A=\sum_{p,q\in\Lambda^{*}}A_{p,q}\,a_{p+q}a_{-p}a_{-q}

(22)

cubic in creation and annihilation operators. The operator
\Theta
, appearing in the cubic transformation, is a cutoff, controlling the local particle density. In the next subsections, we are going to define the kernels
\mu_{p}
,
A_{p,q}
and the cutoff
\Theta
precisely and we are going to study their most important properties.

2.1
Coefficients of the Bogoliubov transformation

We use the solution
f
of the zero-energy scattering equation (
2
) to define the coefficients

\eta_{\infty,p}=\frac{-N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})}{2p^{2}}

(23)

and

\mu_{\infty,p}=-\frac{1}{4}\log(1-4\eta_{\infty,p})

(24)

for
p\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}=2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{3}\backslash\{0\}
. We also define
\eta_{\infty,0}=\mu_{\infty,0}=0
. Notice that

\tanh(2\mu_{\infty,p})=-\frac{\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})}{p^{2}+\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})}\,.

Hence, a Bogoliubov transformation with coefficients
\mu_{\infty,p}
would exactly diagonalize a quadratic Hamiltonian with interaction
Vf
instead of
V
. To describe the action of such a Bogoliubov transformation, we define the coefficients
\sigma_{\infty,p}=\sinh\mu_{\infty,p}
and
\gamma_{\infty,p}=\cosh\mu_{\infty,p}
. We have

\begin{split}&\sigma_{\infty,p}^{2}=\frac{p^{2}+N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})-\sqrt{p^{4}+2p^{2}N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})}}{2\sqrt{p^{4}+2p^{2}N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})}}=\frac{1-2\eta_{\infty,p}-\sqrt{1-4\eta_{\infty,p}}}{2\sqrt{1-4\eta_{\infty,p}}},\\
&\gamma_{\infty,p}^{2}=\frac{p^{2}+N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})+\sqrt{p^{4}+2p^{2}N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})}}{2\sqrt{p^{4}+2p^{2}N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})}}=\frac{1-2\eta_{\infty,p}+\sqrt{1-4\eta_{\infty,p}}}{2\sqrt{1-4\eta_{\infty,p}}},\\
&\gamma_{\infty,p}\sigma_{\infty,p}=\frac{-N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})}{2\sqrt{p^{4}+2p^{2}N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})}}=\frac{\eta_{\infty,p}}{\sqrt{1-4\eta_{\infty,p}}}.\end{split}

(25)

Some useful properties of the coefficients
\eta_{\infty,p}
,
\sigma_{\infty,p}
and
\gamma_{\infty,p}
are collected in the following lemma.

Lemma 3
.

The scattering equation (
2
) translates into the discrete version

p^{2}\eta_{\infty,p}+\frac{N^{\kappa}}{2}\hat{V}(p/N^{1-\kappa})+\frac{N^{\kappa}}{2N}\sum_{q\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\hat{V}((p-q)/N^{1-\kappa})\eta_{\infty,q}=O(N^{2\kappa-1})

(26)

uniformly in
p\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}
. Furthermore,

\sum_{p\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}N^{\kappa}\hat{V}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\eta_{\infty,p}=(8\pi\mathfrak{a}-\hat{V}(0))N^{1+\kappa}+O(N^{2\kappa}).

(27)

The coefficients
\gamma_{\infty,p}
and
\sigma_{\infty,p}
satisfy

\lvert\gamma_{\infty,p}-1\rvert\leq\lvert\sigma_{\infty,p}\rvert\leq C\lvert\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\rvert\,\min\Big(\frac{N^{\kappa/4}}{\lvert p\rvert^{1/2}},\frac{N^{\kappa}}{\lvert p\rvert^{2}}\Big),

(28)

and therefore, for
s\in(3/2,6)
,

\sum_{p\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\lvert\gamma_{\infty,p}-1\rvert^{s}\leq\sum_{p\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\lvert\sigma_{\infty,p}\rvert^{s}\leq CN^{3\kappa/2}

(29)

as well as

\sum_{p\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\lvert\gamma_{\infty,p}-1\rvert\leq\sum_{p\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\lvert\sigma_{\infty,p}\rvert\leq CN.

(30)

We also have

\lvert\gamma_{\infty,p}\sigma_{\infty,p}-\eta_{\infty,p}\rvert\leq C\min\Big(\frac{N^{\kappa}}{\lvert p\rvert^{2}},\frac{N^{2\kappa}}{\lvert p\rvert^{4}}\Big)

(31)

and therefore

\sum_{p\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\lvert\gamma_{\infty,p}\sigma_{\infty,p}-\eta_{\infty,p}\rvert\leq CN^{3\kappa/2}.

(32)

Before proving Lemma
3
, we establish some preliminary bounds.

Lemma 4
.

For
m\in\mathbb{N}
, let

F_{m}(\xi)=\Big(1+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{3}:|k|\leq m}\lvert\mathcal{F}[x^{k}Vf](\xi)\rvert]\Big)^{m}-1.

(33)

Then, for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
there is a constant
C_{m}>0
such that

\lVert F_{m}\rVert_{\infty}\leq C_{m}

(34)

and

\begin{split}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}F_{m}^{2}((p+\zeta)/N^{1-\kappa})\leq C_{m}N^{3-3\kappa}\end{split}

(35)

for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
and all
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Here,
\mathcal{F}
denotes the Fourier transform on the torus but since
Vf
has compact support, it also equals the one on full space. Moreover, for
k=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
, we use the standard notation
|k|=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}
and
x^{k}=x_{1}^{k_{1}}x_{2}^{k_{2}}x_{3}^{k_{3}}
.

Proof.

The bound (
34
) follows immediately from
\lvert\mathcal{F}[x^{k}Vf](\xi)\rvert\leq C
. Also, using this inequality we have

\begin{split}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}F_{m}^{2}((p+\zeta)/N^{1-\kappa})&\leq C_{m}\sum_{|k|\leq m}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\lvert\mathcal{F}[x^{k}Vf]((p+\zeta)/N^{1-\kappa})\rvert^{2}\\
&\leq C_{m}\sum_{|k|\leq m}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\lvert N^{3-3\kappa}\mathcal{F}[(N^{1-\kappa}\cdot)^{k}\exp(-i\zeta\cdot)Vf(N^{1-\kappa}\cdot)](p)\rvert^{2}\\
&\leq C_{m}N^{3-3\kappa}\sum_{|k|\leq m}\int_{\Lambda}dxN^{3-3\kappa}\lvert(N^{1-\kappa}x)^{k}\exp(-i\zeta x)Vf(N^{1-\kappa}x)\rvert^{2}\\
&\leq C_{m}N^{3-3\kappa}\end{split}

showing (
35
).
∎

We are now ready to show Lemma
3
.

Proof of Lemma
3
.

We define
w=1-f=(-\Delta)^{-1}Vf/2
so that
\eta_{\infty,p}=-N^{3\kappa-2}\widehat{w}(p/N^{1-\kappa})
. For
p\not=0
, we obtain

\begin{split}&p^{2}\eta_{\infty,p}+\frac{N^{\kappa}}{2}\hat{V}(\frac{p}{N^{1-\kappa}})+\frac{N^{\kappa}}{2N}\sum_{q\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\hat{V}(\frac{p-q}{N^{1-\kappa}})\eta_{\infty,q}\\
=&\frac{N^{\kappa}}{2}\widehat{Vw}(\frac{p}{N^{1-\kappa}})+\frac{N^{\kappa}}{2N}\sum_{q\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\hat{V}(\frac{p-q}{N^{1-\kappa}})\eta_{\infty,q}\\
=&\frac{N^{\kappa}}{2(2\pi)^{3}}\int dq\hat{V}(\frac{p}{N^{1-\kappa}}-q)\widehat{w}(q)+\frac{N^{\kappa}}{2N}\sum_{q\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\hat{V}(\frac{p-q}{N^{1-\kappa}})\eta_{\infty,q}\\
=&\frac{N^{\kappa}}{4N(2\pi)^{3}}\int dq\hat{V}(\frac{p-q}{N^{1-\kappa}})\frac{N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(\frac{q}{N^{1-\kappa}})}{q^{2}}-\frac{N^{\kappa}}{4N}\sum_{q\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\hat{V}(\frac{p-q}{N^{1-\kappa}})\frac{N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(\frac{q}{N^{1-\kappa}})}{q^{2}}\\
=&\frac{N^{2\kappa-1}}{4(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{q\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\int_{[-\pi,\pi]^{3}}d\zeta\left(\hat{V}(\frac{p-q+\zeta}{N^{1-\kappa}})\frac{\widehat{Vf}(\frac{q+\zeta}{N^{1-\kappa}})}{(q+\zeta)^{2}}-\hat{V}(\frac{p-q}{N^{1-\kappa}})\frac{\widehat{Vf}(\frac{q}{N^{1-\kappa}})}{q^{2}}\right)\\
&+O(N^{2\kappa-1})\end{split}

(36)

where the error
O(N^{2\kappa-1})
arises from the contribution associated with
q=0
. Hence

\begin{split}&p^{2}\eta_{\infty,p}+\frac{N^{\kappa}}{2}\hat{V}(p/N^{1-\kappa})+\frac{N^{\kappa}}{2N}\sum_{q\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\hat{V}((p-q)/N^{1-\kappa})\eta_{\infty,q}\\
&=\frac{N^{2\kappa-1}}{4(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{q\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\int_{[-\pi,\pi]^{3}}d\zeta\int_{0}^{1}ds\frac{d}{ds}\left(\hat{V}(\frac{p-q+s\zeta}{N^{1-\kappa}})\frac{\widehat{Vf}(\frac{q+s\zeta}{N^{1-\kappa}})}{(q+s\zeta)^{2}}\right)+O(N^{2\kappa-1})\end{split}

and, since the
s
-derivative evaluated at
s=0
is odd in
\zeta
,

\begin{split}&p^{2}\eta_{\infty,p}+\frac{N^{\kappa}}{2}\hat{V}(p/N^{1-\kappa})+\frac{N^{\kappa}}{2N}\sum_{q\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\hat{V}((p-q)/N^{1-\kappa})\eta_{\infty,q}\\
&=\frac{N^{2\kappa-1}}{4(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{q\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\int_{[-\pi,\pi]^{3}}d\zeta\int_{0}^{1}ds\int_{0}^{s}dt\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}\left(\hat{V}(\frac{p-q+t\zeta}{N^{1-\kappa}})\frac{\widehat{Vf}(\frac{q+t\zeta}{N^{1-\kappa}})}{(q+t\zeta)^{2}}\right)+O(N^{2\kappa-1}).\end{split}

(37)

Using the function
F_{2}
, defined in (
33
), we can bound

\begin{split}\sum_{q\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\int_{[-\pi,\pi]^{3}}&d\zeta\int_{0}^{1}ds\int_{0}^{s}dt\;\Big|\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}\left(\hat{V}(\frac{p-q+t\zeta}{N^{1-\kappa}})\frac{\widehat{Vf}(\frac{q+t\zeta}{N^{1-\kappa}})}{(q+t\zeta)^{2}}\right)\Big|\\
\leq\;&C\sum_{q\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\int_{[-\pi,\pi]^{3}}d\zeta\int_{0}^{1}ds\int_{0}^{s}dt\,F_{2}(\frac{q+t\zeta}{N^{1-\kappa}})\sum_{n=0}^{2}N^{-(1-\kappa)(2-n)}\lvert q+t\zeta\rvert^{-2-n}\\
\leq\;&C\int_{[-\pi,\pi]^{3}}d\zeta\int_{0}^{1}ds\int_{0}^{s}dt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}\\
\lvert q+t\zeta\rvert\leq N^{1-\kappa}\end{subarray}}\lVert F_{2}\rVert_{\infty}\sum_{n=0}^{2}N^{-(1-\kappa)(2-n)}\lvert q+t\zeta\rvert^{-2-n}\\
&+C\int_{[-\pi,\pi]^{3}}d\zeta\int_{0}^{1}ds\int_{0}^{s}dt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}\\
\lvert q+t\zeta\rvert>N^{1-\kappa}\end{subarray}}F_{2}(\frac{q+t\zeta}{N^{1-\kappa}})N^{-2(1-\kappa)}\lvert q+t\zeta\rvert^{-2}\\
\leq\;&C.\end{split}

To bound the sum over
|q+t\zeta|>N^{1-\kappa}
, we used Cauchy-Schwarz and (
35
).
From (
37
), we obtain (
26
), uniformly in
p\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}
.

To prove (
27
) we observe that, starting from the second line, (
36
) also holds for
p=0
. This implies that

N^{1+\kappa}\widehat{Vw}(0)+N^{\kappa}\sum_{q\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}}\hat{V}((p-q/N^{1-\kappa})\eta_{q}=O(N^{2\kappa}).

With
\widehat{Vw}(0)=\hat{V}(0)-\widehat{Vf}(0)=\hat{V}(0)-8\pi\mathfrak{a}
, we obtain (
27
).

The bounds (
28
) to (
32
) are easy to check using the explicit form in (
25
).
∎

Let
\beta=2-3\kappa>0
. Noticing with (
28
) that

N^{\kappa}\sum_{|p|<N^{\alpha}}\sigma_{\infty,p}^{2}\leq CN^{3\kappa/2+2\alpha}\leq CN^{4\kappa-1}

(38)

if
\alpha\leq\kappa/2-\beta/4
, we conclude that excitations with momentum below
N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}
are negligible, to resolve the energy to the precision we are aiming for (the l.h.s. of (
38
) arises from the quadratic term
N^{\kappa}\sum_{p}\hat{V}(p/N^{1-\kappa})a_{p}^{*}a_{p}
, after conjugation with the Bogoliubov transformation; also the contribution of the other quadratic terms in the excitation Hamiltonian is negligible, for
|p|<N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}
). Thus, we can impose an infrared cutoff on the coefficients
\mu_{p}
of the Bogoliubov transformation defined in (
21
). In order to guarantee decay in position space, it is important to use a smooth cutoff. We choose
\chi_{l}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
with
0\leq\chi_{l}\leq 1
,
\chi_{l}(x)=0
if
x\leq 1
,
\chi_{l}(x)=1
if
x\geq 2
, and we set
\chi(p)=\chi_{l}(\lvert p\rvert/N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4})
. For
p\in\Lambda^{*}
, we define

\mu_{p}\coloneqq\mu_{\infty,p}\chi(p)

(39)

with
\mu_{\infty,p}
as in (
24
). With these coefficients we set

B=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\mu_{p}a_{p}a_{-p}\,.

The trial state (
18
) is defined through the Bogoliubov transformation
e^{B-B^{*}}
, whose action on creation and annihilation operators is determined by

e^{B-B^{*}}a_{p}e^{B^{*}-B}=\gamma_{p}a_{p}+\sigma_{p}a_{-p}^{*},\qquad e^{B-B^{*}}a^{*}_{p}e^{B^{*}-B}=\gamma_{p}a^{*}_{p}+\sigma_{p}a_{-p}

(40)

where, similarly as in (
25
), we introduced the notation
\sigma_{p}=\sinh\mu_{p}
,
\gamma_{p}=\cosh\mu_{p}
, for all
p\in\Lambda^{*}
. In position space, we set

\check{\sigma}(x)=\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\sigma_{p}e^{ip\cdot x},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\widecheck{\gamma-1}(x)=\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}(\gamma_{p}-1)e^{ip\cdot x}.

We will also make use of continuous versions of the coefficients
\mu_{p},\sigma_{p},\gamma_{p}
. Analogously to (
23
), (
24
), we define therefore, for every
p\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\eta_{\infty}(p)=-N^{\kappa}\frac{\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})}{2p^{2}},\quad\mu_{\infty}(p)=-\frac{1}{4}\log(1-4\eta_{\infty}(p)),

as well as
\sigma_{\infty}(p)=\sinh\mu_{\infty}(p)
,
\gamma_{\infty}(p)=\cosh\mu_{\infty}(p)
and
\mu(p)=\mu_{\infty}(p)\chi(p)
,
\sigma(p)=\sinh\mu(p)
and
\gamma(p)=\cosh\mu(p)
. Notice that

\mu(p)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\textrm{ if }\lvert p\rvert\leq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4},\\
\mu_{\infty}(p)&\textrm{ if }\lvert p\rvert\geq 2N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}\end{array}\right.

(41)

and also that
|\mu(p)|\leq|\mu_{\infty}(p)|
for all
p\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
. This implies that
|\sigma(p)|\leq|\sigma_{\infty}(p)|
,
0\leq\gamma(p)-1\leq\gamma_{\infty}(p)-1
,
\sigma(p)=0
and
\gamma(p)=1
for
|p|<N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}
.

In the next lemma, we collect various properties of the coefficients
\sigma_{p},\gamma_{p}
and of the corresponding Fourier series.

Lemma 5
.

In momentum space we have the bounds

\lvert\gamma(p)-1\rvert\leq\lvert\sigma(p)\rvert\leq C\lvert\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\rvert\min(N^{\kappa/4}\lvert p\rvert^{-1/2},N^{\kappa}\lvert p\rvert^{-2})

(42)

which imply

\lVert\sigma\rVert_{\infty},\lVert\gamma-1\rVert_{\infty}\leq CN^{\beta/8},

(43)

\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\lvert\sigma_{p}\rvert,\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\lvert\gamma_{p}-1\rvert\leq CN

(44)

and, for
s\in(3/2,6)
,

\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\lvert\gamma_{p}-1\rvert^{s}\leq\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\lvert\sigma_{p}\rvert^{s}\leq CN^{3\kappa/2}.

(45)

Furthermore, we have the following bounds in position space:

\lvert\check{\sigma}(x)\rvert,\lvert\widecheck{\gamma-1}(x)\rvert\leq CN

(46)

\lvert\check{\sigma}(x)\rvert,\lvert\widecheck{\gamma-1}(x)\rvert\leq C(\log N)^{2}N^{\kappa}\lvert x\rvert^{-1}

(47)

\lvert\check{\sigma}(x)\rvert,\lvert\widecheck{\gamma-1}(x)\rvert\leq C(\log N)^{3}N^{\kappa/4}\lvert x\rvert^{-5/2}

(48)

and, for any
m\geq 2
, there exists
C_{m}>0
such that

\lvert\check{\sigma}(x)\rvert,\lvert\widecheck{\gamma-1}(x)\rvert\leq C_{m}N\left(\frac{\ell_{\sigma}}{\lvert x\rvert}\right)^{m}

(49)

where
\ell_{\sigma}=N^{-\kappa/2+\beta/4}
.

Remark.
The decay
|x|^{-5/2}
in (
48
) corresponds to the bound proportional to
|p|^{-1/2}
in (
42
).

Proof.

The bounds in momentum space follow similarly to Lemma
3
, since
|\sigma(p)|\leq|\sigma_{\infty}(p)|
,
|\gamma(p)-1|\leq|\gamma_{\infty}(p)-1|
.

To establish the estimates in position space, we study the derivatives of
\mu(p),\sigma(p),\gamma(p)
. First, we observe that
|\mu(p)|\leq C\log N
, for
|p|<N^{\kappa/2}
, and
|\mu(p)|\leq CN^{\kappa}/|p|^{2}
, for
|p|>N^{\kappa/2}
. Hence, we have

\lvert\mu(p)\rvert\leq C(\log N)\frac{N^{\kappa}}{N^{\kappa}+p^{2}}

(50)

for all
p\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Also,

\partial_{p_{j}}\mu_{\infty}(p)=-\frac{1}{4}\left(1+\frac{2N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(\frac{p}{N^{1-\kappa}})}{p^{2}}\right)^{-1}\partial_{p_{j}}\left(\frac{2N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(\frac{p}{N^{1-\kappa}})}{p^{2}}\right)\,.

(51)

Thus, recalling the definition (
33
), we find, for any integer
m\geq 1
,

\begin{split}\lvert\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}\mu_{\infty}(p)\rvert&\leq C_{m}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\Big|1+\frac{2N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(\frac{p}{N^{1-\kappa}})}{p^{2}}\Big|^{-k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\geq 1:\\
\sum_{i=1}^{k}m_{i}=m\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\Big|\partial_{p_{j}}^{m_{i}}\left(\frac{N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(\frac{p}{N^{1-\kappa}})}{p^{2}}\right)\Big|\\
&\leq C_{m}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(\frac{p^{2}}{p^{2}+N^{\kappa}}\right)^{k}F_{m}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\geq 1:\\
\sum_{i=1}^{k}m_{i}=m\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{n_{i}=0}^{m_{i}}N^{\kappa}\lvert p\rvert^{-2-n_{i}}N^{-(1-\kappa)(m_{i}-n_{i})}.\end{split}

Setting
n=n_{1}+\dots+n_{k}
and absorbing combinatorial factors from the sums over
m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\geq 1
with
m_{1}+\dots+m_{k}=m
and over
n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\geq 0
with
n_{1}+\dots+n_{k}=n\leq m
, we arrive at

\begin{split}\lvert\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}\mu_{\infty}(p)\rvert&\leq C_{m}F_{m}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\frac{N^{\kappa}}{p^{2}+N^{\kappa}}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\lvert p\rvert^{-n}N^{-(1-\kappa)(m-n)}\\
&\leq C_{m}F_{m}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\frac{N^{\kappa}}{p^{2}+N^{\kappa}}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\lvert p\rvert^{-n}N^{-(1-\kappa)(m-n)}.\end{split}

(52)

Recalling that
\chi(p)=\chi_{l}(|p|/N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4})
, we find, for integer
m\geq 1
,

\begin{split}\lvert\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}\chi(p)\rvert&\leq C_{m}N^{-m(\kappa/2-\beta/4)}\mathbbm{1}_{[N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4},2N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}]}(\lvert p\rvert)\\
&\leq C_{m}\lvert p\rvert^{-m}\mathbbm{1}_{[N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4},2N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}]}(\lvert p\rvert)\,.\end{split}

(53)

Combining this bound with (
52
), we obtain, for
m\geq 0
,

\lvert\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}\mu(p)\rvert\leq C_{m}F_{m}(p/N^{1-\kappa})(\log N)\frac{N^{\kappa}}{N^{\kappa}+p^{2}}\mathbbm{1}(\lvert p\rvert\geq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4})\sum_{n=0}^{m}\lvert p\rvert^{-n}N^{-(1-\kappa)(m-n)}

where the factor
(\log N)
comes from (
50
), to handle the contribution in which all derivatives hit
\chi
. We apply now the last bound to control derivatives of
\sigma(p),\gamma(p)
. For
m\geq 0
, we obtain

\begin{split}\lvert\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}\sigma(p)\rvert\leq\;&C_{m}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\lvert\sinh^{(k)}(\mu(p))\rvert\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\geq 1:\\
\sum_{i=1}^{k}m_{i}=m\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\lvert\partial_{p_{j}}^{m_{i}}\mu(p)\rvert\\
\leq\;&C_{m}\Big(1+\min\Big(\frac{N^{\kappa/4}}{\lvert p\rvert^{1/2}},\frac{N^{\kappa}}{\lvert p\rvert^{2}}\Big)\Big)(\log N)^{m}\frac{N^{\kappa}}{N^{\kappa}+p^{2}}\,F_{m}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\times\sum_{n=0}^{m}\lvert p\rvert^{-n}N^{-(1-\kappa)(m-n)}\mathbbm{1}(\lvert p\rvert\geq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4})\end{split}

where we used (
42
) to estimate
|\sinh^{(k)}(\mu(p))|
and the bound
\prod_{j=1}^{k}F_{m_{j}}(p)\leq F_{m}(p)
, if
m_{1}+\dots+m_{k}=m
(several combinatorial factors, arising from sums over
m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\geq 1
with fixed
m=m_{1}+\dots+m_{k}
and also from sums over
n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\geq 0
with fixed
n_{1}+\dots+n_{k}=n
have been absorbed in the constant
C_{m}
). Distinguishing
|p|>N^{\kappa/2}
and
|p|\leq N^{\kappa/2}
and using the factor
N^{\kappa}/(N^{\kappa}+p^{2})
as well as using Young’s inequality for the sum over
n
, we obtain

\begin{split}\lvert\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}\sigma(p)\rvert\leq\;&C_{m}(\log N)^{m}\min\Big(\frac{N^{\kappa/4}}{\lvert p\rvert^{1/2}},\frac{N^{\kappa}}{\lvert p\rvert^{2}}\Big)F_{m}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\mathbbm{1}(\lvert p\rvert\geq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4})\\
&\hskip 199.16928pt\times\big(|p|^{-m}+N^{-m(1-\kappa)}\big).\end{split}

(54)

Analogously, we find

\begin{split}\lvert\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}\gamma(p)\rvert&\leq C_{m}(\log N)^{m}\min\Big(\frac{N^{\kappa/4}}{\lvert p\rvert^{1/2}},\frac{N^{\kappa}}{\lvert p\rvert^{2}}\Big)F_{m}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\mathbbm{1}(\lvert p\rvert\geq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4})\\
&\hskip 199.16928pt\times\big(|p|^{-m}+N^{-m(1-\kappa)}\big)\,.\end{split}

Now we apply these bounds to show the pointwise bounds in position space.
The bounds (
46
) follow immediately from (
44
).
Next, we observe that, for
x\in\Lambda=[-1/2;1/2]^{3}
and any integer
m\geq 1
, we have

\begin{split}c^{m}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{m}\lvert\check{\sigma}(x)\rvert&\leq\lvert(e^{2\pi ix_{j}}-1)^{m}\check{\sigma}(x)\rvert=\lvert(e^{2\pi ix_{j}}-1)^{m}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\sigma(p)e^{ip\cdot x}\rvert\\
&=\lvert(e^{2\pi ix_{j}}-1)^{m-1}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}(\sigma(p-2\pi e_{j})-\sigma(p))e^{ip\cdot x}\rvert\\
&=\Big|\int_{0}^{2\pi}ds\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}(e^{2\pi ix_{j}}-1)^{m-1}\partial_{p_{j}}\sigma(p-se_{j})e^{ip\cdot x}\Big|\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{2\pi}ds_{1}\dots\int_{0}^{2\pi}ds_{m}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\Big|\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}\sigma(p-\sum_{i=1}^{m}s_{i}e_{j})\Big|\\
&\leq(2\pi)^{m-1}\int_{0}^{2\pi m}ds\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\lvert\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}\sigma(p-se_{j})\rvert.\end{split}

(55)

Taking
m=1
and using (
54
), we find

\begin{split}\lvert x_{j}\rvert\lvert\check{\sigma}(x)\rvert&\leq C(\log N)\int_{0}^{2\pi}ds\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}:\\
|p-se_{j}|\geq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}\end{subarray}}\frac{N^{\kappa}(N^{-(1-\kappa)}+\lvert p-se_{j}\rvert^{-1})}{\lvert p-se_{j}\rvert^{2}}\\
&\hskip 199.16928pt\times F_{1}((p-se_{j})/N^{1-\kappa}).\end{split}

We divide the sum into the two regions
N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}\leq|p-se_{j}|\leq N^{1-\kappa}
and
|p-se_{j}|>N^{1-\kappa}
. In the first region, we bound
F_{1}
in the
\ell^{\infty}
-norm. In the second region, we use instead the
\ell^{2}
-norm of
F_{1}
. With (
35
), we obtain

\lvert x_{j}\rvert\lvert\check{\sigma}(x)\rvert\leq C(\log N)^{2}N^{\kappa}.

Combining the bounds with different choices of
j
, we obtain

\lvert\check{\sigma}(x)\rvert\leq C(\log N)^{2}N^{\kappa}\lvert x\rvert^{-1}.

(56)

Since the estimate for
\gamma-1
can be shown similarly, this concludes the proof of (
47
).

Next, we prove (
48
). To this end, we can restrict our attention to
|x|\geq N^{-\kappa/2}
(for
|x|<N^{-\kappa/2}
, (
47
) is stronger). We split
\check{\sigma}(x)=\check{\sigma}_{1}(x)+\check{\sigma}_{2}(x)
, with

\check{\sigma}_{1}(x)=\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\sigma(p)(1-\chi_{l}(\lvert p\rvert\lvert x\rvert))e^{ip\cdot x},\qquad\check{\sigma}_{2}(x)=\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\sigma(p)\chi_{l}(\lvert p\rvert\lvert x\rvert)e^{ip\cdot x}.

Recalling that
\sigma(p)=0
for
|p|<N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}
, we have

\lvert\sigma(p)\rvert(1-\chi_{l}(\lvert p\rvert\lvert x\rvert))\leq\lvert\sigma(p)\rvert\mathbbm{1}_{[N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4},2\lvert x\rvert^{-1}]}(\lvert p\rvert).

With the bound (
42
), we immediately obtain

\lvert\check{\sigma}_{1}(x)\rvert\leq C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}:\\
N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}\leq\lvert p\rvert\leq 2\lvert x\rvert^{-1}\end{subarray}}N^{\kappa/4}\lvert p\rvert^{-1/2}\leq CN^{\kappa/4}\lvert x\rvert^{-5/2}.

(57)

As for the second term, note that, for integer
m\geq 0
,

{\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}\chi_{l}(\lvert p\rvert\lvert x\rvert)}\leq C_{m}\lvert p\rvert^{-m}\mathbbm{1}(\lvert p\rvert\geq\lvert x\rvert^{-1}),

(58)

and therefore

\begin{split}\lvert\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}(\sigma(p)\chi_{l}(\lvert p\rvert\lvert x\rvert))\rvert&\leq C_{m}(\log N)^{m}\min\Big(\frac{N^{\kappa/4}}{\lvert p\rvert^{1/2}},\frac{N^{\kappa}}{\lvert p\rvert^{2}}\Big)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times F_{m}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\mathbbm{1}(\lvert p\rvert\geq\lvert x\rvert^{-1})\big(|p|^{-m}+N^{-m(1-\kappa)}\big).\end{split}

(59)

Proceeding as in (
55
), we find

\begin{split}c^{3}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{3}\lvert\check{\sigma}_{2}(x)\rvert\leq\;&(2\pi)^{2}\int_{0}^{6\pi}ds\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\lvert\partial_{p_{j}}^{3}(\sigma\chi_{l}(\cdot\lvert x\rvert))(p-se_{j})\rvert\\
\leq\;&C_{3}(\log N)^{3}\int_{0}^{6\pi}ds\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\in\Lambda^{*}\\
\lvert p-se_{j}\rvert\geq\lvert x\rvert^{-1}\end{subarray}}\frac{N^{\kappa/4}}{\lvert p-se_{j}\rvert^{7/2}}F_{3}((p-se_{j})/N^{1-\kappa})\\
&+C_{3}(\log N)^{3}\int_{0}^{6\pi}ds\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\in\Lambda^{*}\\
\lvert p-se_{j}\rvert\geq|x|^{-1}\end{subarray}}\frac{N^{\kappa}}{|p-se_{j}|^{2}}F_{3}((p-se_{j})/N^{1-\kappa})N^{-3(1-\kappa)}.\end{split}

Estimating
F_{3}
with its
\ell^{\infty}
-norm (in the first term) and its
\ell^{2}
-norm (in the second term) and applying Lemma
4
, we obtain

\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{3}\lvert\check{\sigma}_{2}(x)\rvert\leq C(\log N)^{3}N^{\kappa/4}|x|^{1/2}.

Combined with (
57
), this implies (
48
) (the bound for
1-\gamma
can be shown similarly).

Finally, we show (
49
). Here, it is enough to consider
|x|>\ell_{\sigma}=N^{-\kappa/2+\beta/4}
and
m\geq 2
. Then, we have,

\begin{split}c^{m}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{m}&\lvert\check{\sigma}(x)\rvert\\
\leq\;&(2\pi)^{m}\int_{0}^{2\pi m}ds\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\lvert(\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}\sigma(p-se_{j})\rvert\\
\leq\;&C_{m}(\log N)^{m}\int_{0}^{2\pi m}ds\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\in\Lambda^{*}\\
\lvert p-se_{j}\rvert\geq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}\end{subarray}}\frac{N^{\kappa}}{\lvert p-se_{j}\rvert^{2}}F_{m}((p-se_{j})/N^{1-\kappa})N^{-(1-\kappa)m}\\
&+C_{m}(\log N)^{m}\int_{0}^{2\pi m}ds\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\in\Lambda^{*}\\
\lvert p-se_{j}\rvert\geq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}\end{subarray}}\frac{N^{\kappa}}{\lvert p-se_{j}\rvert^{m+2}}F_{m}((p-se_{j})/N^{1-\kappa}).\end{split}

Estimating
F_{m}
with its
\ell^{2}
-norm in the first term and with its
\ell^{\infty}
-norm in the second term, we conclude that

\begin{split}c^{m}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{m}\lvert\check{\sigma}(x)\rvert&\leq C_{m}(\log N)^{m}NN^{-m(\kappa/2-\beta/4)}.\end{split}

Bounds for
\gamma-1
can be proven analogously; this implies (
49
).
∎

In our analysis of the action of the cubic transformation
e^{A^{*}\Theta-\Theta A}
, it will often be convenient to localize kernels in small boxes, to take advantage of the presence of the cutoff
\Theta
, controlling the local number of particles. Recalling that
\beta=2-3\kappa>0
, we introduce the length scale
\ell_{B}=N^{-\kappa/2+\beta/12}
. We consider the lattice
\Lambda_{B}=\Lambda\cap\ell_{B}\mathbb{Z}^{3}
and, for
u\in\Lambda_{B}
, we define the box
B_{u}=\{x\in\Lambda,\inf_{v\in\Lambda_{B}}\lvert x-v\rvert=\lvert x-u\rvert\}
. We denote by
\check{\sigma}_{u}=\check{\sigma}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{u}}
and
(\widecheck{\gamma-1})_{u}=(\widecheck{\gamma-1})\mathbbm{1}_{B_{u}}
the restrictions of
\check{\sigma}
and
\widecheck{1-\gamma}
on
B_{u}
. Clearly,
\check{\sigma}=\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{B}}\check{\sigma}_{u}
and
\widecheck{\gamma-1}=\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{B}}(\widecheck{\gamma-1})_{u}
.

Lemma 6
.

For every
\epsilon>0
, there is
C>0
such that

\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{B}}\lVert\check{\sigma}_{u}\rVert_{2},\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{B}}\lVert(\widecheck{\gamma-1})_{u}\rVert_{2}\leq CN^{3\kappa/4+\epsilon}

(60)

and

\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{B}}\lVert\check{\sigma}_{u}\rVert_{1},\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{B}}\lVert(\widecheck{\gamma-1})_{u}\rVert_{1}\leq CN^{\beta/8+\epsilon}.

(61)

Remark.
Eq. (
60
) implies that
\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{B}}\|\check{\sigma}_{u}\|_{2}
is comparable (up to the additional factor
N^{\epsilon}
) with
\|\check{\sigma}\|_{2}
. In fact,
\ell_{B}
is the smallest length scale with this property (this is the reason for fixing
\ell_{B}=N^{-\kappa/2+\beta/12}
).

Proof.

We only show the bounds for
\check{\sigma}
, the others follow analogously. For any
\delta>0
, we have

\begin{split}&\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{B}}\lVert\check{\sigma}_{u}\rVert_{2}=\lVert\check{\sigma}_{0}\rVert_{2}+\sum_{0\neq u\in\Lambda_{B},\lvert u\rvert\leq\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}}\lVert\check{\sigma}_{u}\rVert_{2}+\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{B},\lvert u\rvert>\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}}\lVert\check{\sigma}_{u}\rVert_{2}.\end{split}

The first term can be bounded by
\|\check{\sigma}_{0}\|_{2}\leq\|\check{\sigma}\|_{2}\leq CN^{3\kappa/4}
, using (
45
). To estimate the second and third terms, we use the pointwise bounds (
48
) and, respectively, (
49
). We find, for any integer
m\geq 2
,

\begin{split}\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{B}}\lVert\check{\sigma}_{u}\rVert_{2}\leq\;&CN^{3\kappa/4}+C(\log N)^{3}\sum_{0\neq u\in\Lambda_{B},\lvert u\rvert\leq\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}}N^{\kappa/4}\ell_{B}^{3/2}\lvert u\rvert^{-5/2}\\
&+C\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{B},\lvert u\rvert>\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}}NN^{-\delta m}\ell_{B}^{3/2}\\
\leq\;&CN^{3\kappa/4}+C(\log N)^{3}N^{\kappa/4}\ell_{\sigma}^{1/2}N^{\delta/2}\ell_{B}^{-3/2}+CNN^{-\delta m}\ell_{B}^{-3/2}\\
\leq\;&C(\log N)^{3}N^{3\kappa/4}N^{\delta/2}+CNN^{-\delta m}\ell_{B}^{-3/2}.\end{split}

Choosing
\delta
sufficiently small and then
m
sufficiently large, we obtain (
60
). To prove (
61
), we observe that
\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{B}}\|\check{\sigma}_{u}\|_{1}\leq\|\check{\sigma}\|_{1}
and we use the pointwise bounds (
48
) for
|x|\leq N^{-\kappa/2+\beta/4+\delta}
and (
49
) for
|x|>N^{-\kappa/2+\beta/4+\delta}
.
∎

2.2
Coefficients of the Cubic Transformation

In this subsection, we are going to introduce the coefficients
A_{p,q}
, defining the cubic transformation (
22
). We proceed similarly as in
[
9
]
. From (
39
), we recall the cutoff function
\chi_{l}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, satisfying
0\leq\chi_{l}\leq 1
,
\chi_{l}(x)=0
if
x\leq 1
,
\chi_{l}(x)=1
if
x\geq 2
. We define
\tilde{\chi}(p)=\chi_{l}(\lvert p\rvert/N^{\kappa/2})
and, for every
p,q\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash\{0\}
,

A(p,q)=N^{-1/2}\tilde{\chi}(p)\frac{-N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(\frac{p}{N^{1-\kappa}})\sigma(q)}{p^{2}+q^{2}+(p+q)^{2}}=N^{-1/2}\frac{2p^{2}\eta(p)\sigma(q)}{p^{2}+q^{2}+(p+q)^{2}},

(62)

with
\sigma(q)
as defined above (
41
) and

\eta(p)=\frac{-N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(\frac{p}{N^{1-\kappa}})}{2p^{2}}\tilde{\chi}(p)\,.

(63)

Notice that the infrared cutoffs act differently in the two variables. In
p
, we restrict to momenta larger than
N^{\kappa/2}
. In
q
, on the other hand, we have to consider momenta larger than
N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}
. For
p,q\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}
, we also use the notation
\eta_{p}=\eta(p)
and
A_{p,q}=A(p,q)
. For completeness, we set
A_{p,q}=0
, if
p=0
or
q=0
.
In position space, we denote

\check{A}(x,y)=\sum_{p,q\in\Lambda^{*}}A_{p,q}e^{ipx}e^{iqy}.

(64)

Several properties of the coefficients
A_{p,q},\eta_{p}
and of the function
\check{A}
are collected in the next lemma.

Lemma 7
.

First, we list bounds in momentum space. From (
42
), we recall

\lvert\sigma_{p}\rvert\leq C\lvert\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\rvert\min(N^{\kappa/4}\lvert p\rvert^{-1/2},N^{\kappa}\lvert p\rvert^{-2}).

(65)

With the trivial bound

\lvert\eta_{p}\rvert\leq N^{\kappa}\lvert\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\rvert\lvert p\rvert^{-2}

and recalling
\eta_{p}=0
for
|p|<N^{\kappa/2}
, we find

\lVert\eta\rVert_{\infty}\leq C,\quad\lVert\eta\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq CN^{3\kappa/2},\quad\lVert\eta\rVert_{1}\leq CN.

(66)

Moreover

\frac{N^{\kappa}}{N}\sum_{q\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\hat{V}(\frac{p-q}{N^{1-\kappa}})\eta_{q}=-N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vw}(\frac{p}{N^{1-\kappa}})+O(N^{5\kappa/2-1})

(67)

uniformly in
p
. Furthermore, we have the bounds

\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\lvert\eta_{p}-\sigma_{p}\rvert\leq CN^{3\kappa/2},\qquad\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}p^{4}\lvert\eta_{p}-\sigma_{p}\rvert^{2}\leq CN^{7\kappa/2}

(68)

From

\lvert A_{p,q}\rvert\leq 2N^{-1/2}\lvert\eta_{p}\rvert\lvert\sigma_{q}\rvert

(69)

we conclude that

\sum_{p,q\in\Lambda^{*}}\lvert A_{p,q}\rvert^{2}\leq CN^{3\kappa-1}

(70)

and that

\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\lvert A_{p,-p-q}\rvert\leq CN^{2\kappa-1/2}\lvert q\rvert^{-1}.

(71)

In position space, there is
C>0
and, for all
m\geq 2
,
C_{m}>0
such that

\begin{split}\lvert\check{A}(x,y)\rvert\leq&CN^{-1/2}\min\Big(N,(\log N)\frac{N^{\kappa}}{\lvert x\rvert},C_{m}N\Big(\frac{\ell_{\eta}}{\lvert x\rvert}\Big)^{m}\Big)\\
&\times\min\left(N,(\log N)^{2}\frac{N^{\kappa}}{\lvert y\rvert},(\log N)^{3}\frac{N^{\kappa/4}}{\lvert y\rvert^{5/2}},C_{m}N\left(\frac{\ell_{\sigma}}{\lvert y\rvert}\right)^{m}\right)\end{split}

(72)

where
\ell_{\eta}=N^{-\kappa/2}
and
\ell_{\sigma}=N^{-\kappa/2+\beta/4}
.

Proof.

The bounds (
66
), (
69
), (
70
) and (
71
) are easy to check. Eq. (
67
) follows from (
36
) (whose second line is shown to be
O(N^{2\kappa-1})
), because

\frac{N^{\kappa}}{N}\sum_{q\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}}|\eta_{q}-\eta_{\infty,q}|\leq C\frac{N^{\kappa}}{N}\sum_{|q|<2N^{\kappa/2}}\frac{N^{\kappa}}{q^{2}}\leq CN^{5\kappa/2-1}.

To prove (
68
), we observe that, from Lemma
3
,

\begin{split}|\eta_{p}-\sigma_{p}|&\leq|\eta_{p}-\eta_{\infty,p}|+|\sigma_{p}-\sigma_{\infty,p}|+|\eta_{\infty,p}-\gamma_{\infty,p}\sigma_{\infty,p}|+|\sigma_{\infty,p}||\gamma_{\infty,p}-1|\\
&\leq C\min\Big(\frac{N^{\kappa}}{p^{2}},\frac{N^{2\kappa}}{|p|^{4}}\Big).\end{split}

As for the position space bound (
72
), we proceed similarly as in the proof of Lemma
5
. Analogously to (
55
) we find

c^{m}c^{l}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{m}\lvert y_{k}\rvert^{l}\lvert\check{A}(x,y)\rvert\leq C\int_{0}^{2\pi m}ds\int_{0}^{2\pi l}dt\sum_{p,q\in\Lambda^{*}}\lvert(\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}\partial_{q_{k}}^{l}A(p-se_{j},q-te_{k})\rvert

(73)

which again allows to relate position space decay to derivatives in momentum space.
For the derivatives of the kernel we find

\begin{split}\lvert\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}\partial_{q_{k}}^{l}A(p,q)\rvert&\leq C_{m,l}N^{-1/2+\kappa}\\
&\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{i},l_{i}\geq 0\\
m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3}=m\\
l_{1}+l_{2}=l\end{subarray}}\Big|\partial_{p_{j}}^{m_{1}}\tilde{\chi}(p)\partial_{p_{j}}^{m_{2}}\widehat{Vf}(\frac{p}{N^{1-\kappa}})\partial_{p_{j}}^{m_{3}}\partial_{q_{k}}^{l_{1}}\frac{1}{p^{2}+q^{2}+(p+q)^{2}}\partial_{q_{k}}^{l_{2}}\sigma(q)\Big|\end{split}

Recalling the definition (
33
) and the bound (
54
) for the derivatives of
\sigma(q)
, we obtain

\begin{split}\lvert\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}\partial_{q_{k}}^{l}A(p,q)\rvert&\leq C_{m,l}N^{-1/2+\kappa}\\
&\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{i}\geq 0\\
m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3}=m\end{subarray}}\lvert p\rvert^{-(m_{1}+m_{3}+2)}\mathbbm{1}(\lvert p\rvert\geq N^{\kappa/2})N^{-(1-\kappa)m_{2}}F_{m}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\\
&\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{i}\geq 0\\
l_{1}+l_{2}=l\end{subarray}}\lvert q\rvert^{-l_{1}}(\log N)^{l_{2}}\min\Big(\frac{N^{\kappa/4}}{\lvert q\rvert^{1/2}},\frac{N^{\kappa}}{\lvert q\rvert^{2}}\Big)F_{l_{2}}(q/N^{1-\kappa})\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\times\sum_{l_{3}=0}^{l_{2}}\lvert q\rvert^{-l_{3}}N^{-(1-\kappa)(l_{2}-l_{3})}\mathbbm{1}(\lvert q\rvert\geq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}).\end{split}

Setting
n=m_{1}+m_{3}
,
i=l_{1}+l_{3}
(and absorbing appropriate combinatorial factors in the constant
C_{m,l}
), we arrive at

\begin{split}\big|\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}&\partial_{q_{k}}^{l}A(p,q)\big|\\
\leq&C_{m,l}N^{-1/2}\frac{N^{\kappa}}{\lvert p\rvert^{2}}F_{m}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\mathbbm{1}(\lvert p\rvert\geq N^{\kappa/2})\sum_{n=0}^{m}\lvert p\rvert^{-n}N^{-(1-\kappa)(m-n)}\\
&\times(\log N)^{l}\min\Big(\frac{N^{\kappa/4}}{\lvert q\rvert^{1/2}},\frac{N^{\kappa}}{\lvert q\rvert^{2}}\Big)F_{l}(q/N^{1-\kappa})\mathbbm{1}(\lvert q\rvert\geq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4})\sum_{i=0}^{l}\lvert q\rvert^{-i}N^{-(1-\kappa)(l-i)}.\end{split}

Since the r.h.s. is a product of a function of
p
with a function of
q
, we can apply this bound to (
73
) and proceed exactly as in the proof of Lemma
5
, decoupling the variables
x
and
y
.
∎

Similarly as discussed before Lemma
6
for
\check{\sigma}
, it will also be useful to decompose
\check{A}
into a sum of functions
\check{A}_{u,v}
supported in small boxes around the points
u,v\in\Lambda_{B}
(this will allow us to take advantage of the cutoff
\Theta
on the local number of particles).

Lemma 8
.

Recall that
\ell_{B}=N^{-\kappa/2+\beta/12}
,
\Lambda_{B}=\Lambda\cap\ell_{B}\mathbb{Z}^{3}
and that, for
u\in\Lambda_{B}
, we defined
B_{u}=\{x\in\Lambda,\inf_{v\in\Lambda_{B}}\lvert x-v\rvert=\lvert x-u\rvert\}
. We denote by
\check{A}_{u,v}=\check{A}(\mathbbm{1}_{B_{u}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{B_{v}})
the restriction of
\check{A}
to
B_{u}\times B_{v}
, so that
\check{A}=\sum_{u,v\in\Lambda_{B}}\check{A}_{u,v}
. Then, for every
\epsilon>0
, there exists
C>0
such that

\sum_{u,v\in\Lambda_{B}}\lVert\check{A}_{u,v}\rVert_{2}\leq CN^{(3\kappa-1)/2+\epsilon}.

(74)

Proof.

We proceed as in the proof of Lemma
6
, decomposing

\begin{split}\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{B}}\lVert\check{A}_{u,v}\rVert_{2}=\;&\lVert\check{A}_{0,0}\rVert_{2}+\sum_{0\neq v\in\Lambda_{B},\lvert v\rvert\leq\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}}\lVert\check{A}_{0,v}\rVert_{2}+\sum_{v\in\Lambda_{B},\lvert v\rvert>\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}}\lVert\check{A}_{0,v}\rVert_{2}\\
&+\sum_{0\neq u\in\Lambda_{B}}\lVert\check{A}_{u,0}\rVert_{2}+\sum_{0\neq u,v\in\Lambda_{B},\lvert v\rvert\leq\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}}\lVert\check{A}_{u,v}\rVert_{2}+\sum_{0\neq u,v\in\Lambda_{B},\lvert v\rvert>\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}}\lVert\check{A}_{u,v}\rVert_{2}.\end{split}

We estimate
\|\check{A}_{0,0}\|_{2}\leq\|\check{A}\|_{2}\leq CN^{(3\kappa-1)/2}
by (
70
). For the other terms, we use the pointwise bound (
72
). To estimate
\|\check{A}_{0,v}\|_{2}
, for
|v|\leq\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}
, we divide the integral over
x\in B_{0}
in the two regions
|x|\leq\ell_{\eta}N^{\delta}
(where we use the bound for
\check{A}(x,y)
proportional to
N^{\kappa}/|x|
) and
|x|>\ell_{\eta}N^{\delta}
(where we use the arbitrarily fast decay
(\ell_{\eta}/|x|)^{m}
). We find

\begin{split}\|\check{A}_{0,v}\|_{2}&\leq C(\log N)^{4}N^{-1/2+5\kappa/4}|v|^{-5/2}\ell_{B}^{3/2}\Big[\int_{|x|\leq\ell_{\eta}N^{\delta}}|x|^{-2}dx+N^{1-\delta m}\ell_{B}^{3}\Big]^{1/2}\\
&\leq C(\log N)^{4}N^{-1/2+5\kappa/4}|v|^{-5/2}\ell_{B}^{3/2}\big[\ell_{\eta}^{1/2}N^{\delta/2}+N^{(1-\delta m)/2}\ell_{B}^{3/2}\big]\end{split}

for all
|v|\leq\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}
. Hence

\sum_{0\neq v\in\Lambda_{B},|v|\leq\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}}\|\check{A}_{0,v}\|_{2}\leq C(\log N)^{4}N^{-1/2+5\kappa/4}\ell_{\sigma}^{1/2}\ell_{B}^{-3/2}\ell_{\eta}^{1/2}N^{\delta}\leq CN^{(3\kappa-1)/2+2\delta}.

For
|v|>\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}
and also for all terms with
u\not=0
, we can use the arbitrary fast decay of
\check{A}(x,y)
(in
y
, for
|v|>\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}
, and in
x
, if
u\not=0
).
∎

To describe commutators involving the cubic operator (
22
), it is useful to introduce the kernels

\begin{split}\check{A}^{(3)}(x,y)&=\check{A}(x,y)+\check{A}(y,x)+\check{A}(-y,x-y)\\
\check{A}^{(6)}(x,y)&=\check{A}^{(3)}(x,y)+\check{A}^{(3)}(-x,-x+y).\end{split}

(75)

It is sometimes useful to use the notation
\check{A}^{(1)}=\check{A}
. Observe that, by definition,

\check{A}^{(6)}(x,y)=\check{A}^{(6)}(y,x)=\check{A}^{(6)}(x-y,-y)=\check{A}^{(6)}(y-x,-x).

(76)

In Fourier space, we have

\begin{split}A^{(3)}_{p,q}&=A_{p,q}+A_{q,p}+A_{-p-q,p},\\
A^{(6)}_{p,q}&=A^{(3)}_{p,q}+A^{(3)}_{-p-q,q}=A_{p,q}+A_{q,p}+A_{-p-q,p}+A_{-p-q,q}+A_{q,-p-q}+A_{p,-p-q}.\end{split}

(77)

From (
76
), we find

A^{(6)}_{p,q}=A^{(6)}_{q,p}=A^{(6)}_{-p-q,q}=A^{(6)}_{p,-p-q}.

(78)

Additionally, from (
62
), we also have
A^{(j)}_{-p,-q}=A^{(j)}_{p,q}
, for
j=1,3,6
.

We collect some properties of these kernels in the next lemma.

Lemma 9
.

The Fourier coefficients
A^{(j)}_{p,q}
satisfy

\sum_{p,q\in\Lambda^{*}}|A^{(j)}_{p,q}|^{2}\leq CN^{3\kappa-1}

for
j=1,3,6
. Moreover

\begin{split}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}|A_{p,q}^{(j)}|^{2}&\leq CN^{3\kappa/2-1}(1+\sigma_{q}^{2}),\qquad\sum_{q\in\Lambda^{*}}|A_{p,q}^{(j)}|^{2}\leq CN^{3\kappa/2-1}(1+\sigma_{p}^{2})\\
\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}|A_{p,q}^{(j)}|&\leq CN^{1/2}(1+|\sigma_{q}|),\quad\qquad\;\sum_{q\in\Lambda^{*}}|A_{p,q}^{(j)}|\leq CN^{1/2}(1+|\sigma_{p}|)\end{split}

(79)

for
j=1,3,6
. Hence

\sum_{p,q\in\Lambda^{*}}(\sigma_{p}^{2}+\sigma_{q}^{2})|A^{(j)}_{p,q}|^{2}\leq CN^{3\kappa-1}

(80)

for
j=1,3,6
.

As in Lemma
8
, we define
\check{A}^{(j)}_{u,v}=\check{A}^{(j)}\,(\mathbbm{1}_{B_{u}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{B_{v}})
, for
u,v\in\Lambda_{B}=\Lambda\cap\ell_{B}\mathbb{Z}^{3}
and for
j=1,3,6
, so that
\check{A}^{(j)}=\sum_{u,v\in\Lambda_{B}}\check{A}^{(j)}_{u,v}
. Then, we have

\sum_{u,v\in\Lambda_{B}}\|\check{A}^{(j)}_{u,v}\|_{2}\leq CN^{(3\kappa-1)/2+\epsilon}

(81)

for any
\epsilon>0
and for
j=1,3,6
.

It is sometimes also useful to take the supremum in one of the two variables of
\check{A}^{(j)}
.
For
j=1,3,6
, we define
D^{(j)}(x)=\sup_{s}|\check{A}^{(j)}(s,x)|
,
\tilde{D}^{(j)}(x)=\sup_{s}|\check{A}^{(j)}(x,s)|
. Then, we have

\|\tilde{D}^{(j)}\|_{2},\|D^{(j)}\|_{2}\leq CN^{3\kappa/4+1/2+\epsilon}

for any
\epsilon>0
and for
j=1,3,6
. Setting
D_{u}^{(j)}=D^{(j)}\,\mathbbm{1}_{B_{u}}
for
u\in\Lambda_{B}
, we find

\sum_{u\in\Lambda^{*}}\|D^{(j)}_{u}\|_{2}\leq CN^{3\kappa/4+1/2+\epsilon}

for any
\epsilon>0
.

Proof.

The estimates in Fourier space follow from
|A_{p,q}|\leq CN^{-1/2}|\eta_{p}||\sigma_{q}|
and from the estimates for
\eta_{p},\sigma_{q}
in Lemma
5
and Lemma
7
(recalling also that
\eta_{p}=0
, if
|p|<N^{\kappa/2}
, and that
\sigma_{q}=0
if
|q|\leq N^{\kappa/2-\beta/4}
). To show (
80
), we use (
45
).

The bounds (
81
) can be proven proceeding similarly as in the proof of Lemma
8
(it is easy to check that the proof of Lemma
8
also extends to terms like
\check{A}(-y,x-y)\mathbbm{1}(x\in B_{u})\mathbbm{1}(y\in B_{v})
). Finally, the estimates for
D^{(j)},\tilde{D}^{(j)}
and their localized versions follow from the pointwise bound

\begin{split}|D^{(j)}(x)|,|\tilde{D}^{(j)}(x)|\leq\;&CN^{1/2}\min\big(N,(\log N)^{2}\frac{N^{\kappa}}{|x|},(\log N)^{3}\frac{N^{\kappa/4}}{|x|^{5/2}},N(\ell_{\sigma}/|x|)^{m}\big),\end{split}

(82)

valid for
j=1,3,6
, which can be proven with (
72
). To show decay of terms like
\check{A}(-y,x-y)
in the
x
-direction, we use that either
|y|>|x|/2
or
|x-y|>|x|/2
is always true.
∎

The commutator of the cubic transformation (
22
) with the kinetic energy operator is again a cubic operator, with the modified coefficients

A_{\mathcal{K}}(p,q)=-N^{-1/2}N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\tilde{\chi}(p)\sigma(q).

To handle this term, it will be useful to remove the cutoff
\tilde{\chi}
. To this end, we will need to control the expectation of the cubic operator, with coefficients

A_{\mathcal{K}}^{\text{low}}(p,q)=-N^{-1/2}(1-\tilde{\chi}(p))N^{\kappa}\widehat{Vf}(\frac{p}{N^{1-\kappa}})\sigma(q).

(83)

satisfying
A_{\mathcal{K}}^{\text{low}}(p,q)=0
if
|p|>2N^{\kappa/2}
. In the next lemma, we collect some bounds on (
83
), which will be used below, in the proof of Lemma
26
.

Lemma 10
.

We have

\sum_{p,q\in\Lambda^{*}}\lvert A_{\mathcal{K}}^{\text{low}}(p,q)\rvert^{2}\leq CN^{5\kappa-1}.

(84)

Let
\check{A}_{\mathcal{K}}^{\text{low}}(x,y)=\sum_{p,q\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}}A_{\mathcal{K}}^{\text{low}}(p,q)e^{ip\cdot x}e^{iq\cdot y}
. For every
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we find
C_{m}>0
such that

\lvert\check{A}_{\mathcal{K}}^{\text{low}}(x,y)\rvert\leq C_{m}N^{5\kappa/2-1/2}\left(\frac{\ell_{\eta}}{\lvert x\rvert}\right)^{m}\lvert\check{\sigma}(y)\rvert.

(85)

Recall
\ell_{B}=N^{-\kappa/2+\beta/12}
,
\Lambda_{B}=\Lambda\cap\ell_{B}\mathbb{Z}^{3}
. For
u\in\Lambda_{B}
, let
B_{u}=\{x\in\Lambda,\inf_{v\in\Lambda_{B}}\lvert x-v\rvert=\lvert x-u\rvert\}
and denote by
\check{A}_{\mathcal{K},u,v}^{\text{low}}=\check{A}_{\mathcal{K}}^{\text{low}}(\mathbbm{1}_{B_{u}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{B_{v}})
the restriction of
\check{A}_{\mathcal{K}}^{\text{low}}
to
B_{u}\times B_{v}
. Then, for every
\epsilon>0
there is
C>0
such that

\sum_{u,v\in\Lambda_{B}}\lVert\check{A}_{\mathcal{K},u,v}^{\text{low}}\rVert_{2}\leq CN^{(5\kappa-1)/2+\epsilon}.

(86)

Proof.

From (
65
), we find

\sum_{p,q\in\Lambda^{*}}|A_{\mathcal{K}}^{\text{low}}(p,q)|^{2}\leq CN^{-1+2\kappa}\sum_{|p|\leq 2N^{\kappa/2},q\in\Lambda^{*}}|\sigma_{q}|^{2}\leq CN^{5\kappa-1}.

The bound (
85
) follows by noticing that
\check{A}_{\mathcal{K}}^{\text{low}}(x,y)
is the product of a function of
x
(the Fourier series with coefficients
N^{-1/2+\kappa}(1-\tilde{\chi}(p))\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})
) with
\check{\sigma}(y)
and by estimating

\big|\partial^{m}_{p_{j}}\big[(1-\tilde{\chi}(p))\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\big]\big|\leq C_{m}N^{-m\kappa/2}F_{m}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\mathbbm{1}(|p|\leq CN^{\kappa/2})

(where we used the fact that
N^{-1+\kappa}\ll N^{-\kappa/2}
). Thus, proceeding as in (
55
) and recalling
\|F_{m}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{m}
from Lemma
4
, we find

\begin{split}c^{m}|x_{j}|^{m}|\check{A}_{\mathcal{K}}^{\text{low}}(x,y)|&\leq C_{m}N^{-1/2+\kappa}\int_{0}^{2\pi m}ds\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\big|\partial_{p_{j}}^{m}(1-\tilde{\chi}(p))\widehat{Vf}(p/N^{1-\kappa})\big||\check{\sigma}(y)|\\
&\leq C_{m}N^{5\kappa/2-1/2}N^{-m\kappa/2}|\check{\sigma}(y)|\end{split}

which implies (
85
). With (
85
), the bound (
86
) follows, proceeding similarly as in the proof of Lemma
8
. The main contributions to the sum are
\|\check{A}_{\mathcal{K},0,0}^{\text{low}}\|_{2}\leq\|\check{A}_{\mathcal{K}}^{\text{low}}\|_{2}\leq CN^{5\kappa-1}
and the sum over
|v|\leq\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}
of
\|\check{A}_{\mathcal{K},0,v}^{\text{low}}\|_{2}
. Here, we integrate separately over
|x|\leq\ell_{\eta}N^{\delta}
(where we estimate
|\check{A}_{\mathcal{K}}^{\text{low}}(x,y)|\leq CN^{(5\kappa-1)/2}|\check{\sigma}(y)|
) and over
|x|>\ell_{\eta}N^{\delta}
(where we bound
|\check{A}_{\mathcal{K}}^{\text{low}}(x,y)|\leq CN^{(5\kappa-1)/2-\delta m}|\check{\sigma}(y)|
). Using (
48
) to control
|\check{\sigma}(y)|
, we obtain

\begin{split}\sum_{v\in\Lambda_{B},|v|\leq\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}}&\|\check{A}_{\mathcal{K},0,v}^{\text{low}}\|_{2}\\
&\leq C(\log N)^{3}N^{(5\kappa-1)/2}N^{\kappa/4}\ell_{B}^{3/2}\left[\ell_{\eta}^{3}N^{3\delta}+N^{-\delta m}\ell_{B}^{3}\right]^{1/2}\sum_{v\in\Lambda_{B},|v|\leq\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}}|v|^{-5/2}\\
&\leq C(\log N)^{3}N^{(5\kappa-1)/2}N^{\kappa/4}\ell_{B}^{-3/2}\ell_{\eta}^{3/2}\ell_{\sigma}^{1/2}N^{2\delta}\leq CN^{(5\kappa-1)/2+\epsilon}\end{split}

for an arbitrarily small
\epsilon>0
(where we first chose
\delta>0
small enough and then
m\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large). All other contributions can be bounded using the fast decay for
|x|>\ell_{\eta}N^{\delta}
or for
|y|>\ell_{\sigma}N^{\delta}
.
∎

2.3
The Local Particle Number Cutoff

We now define the cutoff
\Theta
to be inserted in the cubic transformation appearing in (
18
) to control the local density of the excitations. We consider the length scale
\ell_{B}=N^{-\kappa/2+\beta/12}
, with
\beta=2-3\kappa
(the choice of
\ell_{B}
is explained in the remark after Lemma
6
). Through
\Theta
, we will require that balls of radius
\ell_{B}
only contain
N^{\epsilon}
particles, for a small
\epsilon>0
. Note that the typical number of cubic excitations in a ball with radius
\ell_{B}
is of the order
\ell_{B}^{3}N^{3\kappa-1}\simeq N^{-\beta/4}\ll 1
(we will show that the total number of cubic excitations is of the order
\|A\|_{2}^{2}\simeq N^{3\kappa-1}
). For this reason,
\Theta
will not substantially change the energy of our trial state.

Pick a radial function
\chi_{\ell_{B}}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
such that
0\leq\chi_{\ell_{B}}\leq 1
,
\chi_{\ell_{B}}(x)=1
if
\lvert x\rvert\leq\ell_{B}
and
\chi_{\ell_{B}}(x)=0
if
\lvert x\rvert\geq 2\ell_{B}
. Define

\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)\coloneqq\int dv\,\chi_{\ell_{B}}(w-v)a_{v}^{*}a_{v}.

(87)

For
\ell>0
, we also define the operator

\mathcal{N}_{\ell}(w)\coloneqq\int dv\mathbbm{1}(\lvert w-v\rvert\leq\ell)\,a_{v}^{*}a_{v}

(88)

counting the number of particles in a ball of radius
\ell
around
w
. We think of
\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)
as a smoothed out version of
\mathcal{N}_{\ell_{B}}(w)
. For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we set

\mathcal{L}_{n}\coloneqq\int dw\,(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+1)^{n}.

(89)

Choosing
\Theta\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
monotonically decreasing with
0\leq\Theta\leq 1
,
\Theta(x)=1
if
x\leq 1
and
\Theta(x)=0
if
x\geq 2
, and for
\alpha>0
to be specified later on, we define the cutoff

\Theta(\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})\,.

Note that

[\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w),a_{x}^{*}]=\chi_{\ell_{B}}(w-x)a_{x}^{*}.

Hence, for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there is a
C_{k}>0
such that

C_{k}^{-1}\lVert\prod_{i=1}^{k}a_{x_{i}}(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+1)\zeta\rVert\leq\lVert(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+1)\prod_{i=1}^{k}a_{x_{i}}\zeta\rVert\leq\lVert\prod_{i=1}^{k}a_{x_{i}}(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+1)\zeta\rVert.

(90)

With the cutoff
\Theta(\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})
, we introduce the unitary cubic transformation

\exp\Big(\int dxdydz\check{A}(x-y,x-z)a_{x}^{*}a_{y}^{*}a_{z}^{*}\Theta(\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})-\text{h.c.}\ \Big)\eqqcolon\exp(A^{*}\Theta-\text{h.c.}\ )

(91)

with the kernel
\check{A}
defined in (
64
).

Lemma 11
.

There is a constant
C>0
such that, for every
\epsilon>0
and
\alpha>0
, and
T>0
, we have

\mathcal{N}_{k\ell_{B}}(w)\Theta(T^{-n}\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})\leq CTk^{3}N^{\epsilon}\Theta(T^{-n}\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})

(92)

and also

\lVert\mathcal{N}_{k\ell_{B}}^{1/2}(w)\prod_{j=1}^{m}a_{x_{j}}\Theta(T^{-n}\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})\zeta\rVert^{2}\leq CTk^{3}N^{\epsilon}\lVert\prod_{j=1}^{m}a_{x_{j}}\Theta(T^{-n}\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})\zeta\rVert^{2}

(93)

for all
n
large enough,
w\in\Lambda
,
k\geq 1
,
\zeta\in\mathcal{F}(\Lambda)
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
Moreover, for every
\alpha>0
, we have

\langle\exp(A^{*}\Theta-\text{h.c.}\ )\Omega,\mathcal{L}_{n}\exp(A^{*}\Theta-\text{h.c.}\ )\Omega\rangle\leq C.

(94)

for all
n
large enough. As a consequence

\lVert(1-\Theta(\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha}))\exp(A^{*}\Theta-\text{h.c.}\ )\Omega\rVert\leq CN^{-\alpha/2}.

(95)

Also, for every
\alpha>0
we have the (non-optimal) bound

\langle\exp(s(A^{*}\Theta-\Theta A))\zeta,\mathcal{N}^{m}\exp(s(A^{*}\Theta-\Theta A))\zeta\rangle\leq CN^{m-m\beta/2}+\langle\zeta,\mathcal{N}^{m}\zeta\rangle

(96)

for all
n
large enough and all normalized
\zeta\in\mathcal{F}(\Lambda)
and
s\in[0,1]
.

Finally, we observe that, for any
\nu>0
, we have

\langle\exp(A^{*}\Theta-\text{h.c.}\ )\Omega,A^{*}(1-\Theta(\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha}))A\exp(A^{*}\Theta-\text{h.c.}\ )\Omega\rangle\leq CN^{-\nu}

(97)

if
\alpha>0
and then
n\in\mathbb{N}
are chosen large enough.

Remark.
As it is clear from their proof, the bounds (
95
) and (
97
) remain true if we replace
1-\Theta
by a sharp cutoff, ie.

\begin{split}\|\mathbbm{1}_{[k,\infty)}(\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})\exp(A^{*}\Theta-\text{h.c.}\ )\Omega\|&\leq CN^{-\alpha/2}\\
\langle\exp(A^{*}\Theta-\text{h.c.}\ )\Omega,A^{*}\mathbbm{1}_{[k,\infty)}(\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})A\exp(A^{*}\Theta-\text{h.c.}\ )\Omega\rangle&\leq N^{-\nu}\end{split}

for
k>0
.
In fact, the last bounds also holds, if we include factors of
(\mathcal{N}+1)
, ie.

\langle\exp(A^{*}\Theta-\text{h.c.}\ )\Omega,A^{*}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{m}\mathbbm{1}_{[k,\infty)}(\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})A\exp(A^{*}\Theta-\text{h.c.}\ )\Omega\rangle\leq N^{-\nu}

if
\alpha
and
n
are chosen large enough (depending on
m
).

Proof.

We begin with (
92
). For arbitrary
w
and
y
with
\lvert w-y\rvert\leq\ell_{B}/2
we have

(\mathcal{N}_{\ell_{B}/2}(w)+1)^{n}\leq(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(y)+1)^{n}.

Hence

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{n}&=\int dy\,(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(y)+1)^{n}\geq\int_{|w-y|\leq\ell_{B}/2}dy\,(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(y)+1)^{n}\\
&\geq\int_{|w-y|\leq\ell_{B}/2}dy\,(\mathcal{N}_{\ell_{B}/2}(w)+1)^{n}\geq C\ell_{B}^{3}(\mathcal{N}_{\ell_{B}/2}(w)+1)^{n}.\end{split}

Taking the
n
-th root and writing
\mathcal{N}_{\ell_{B}}(w)
as the sum over the number of particles in finitely many balls of radius
\ell_{B}/2
, we obtain that, on the support of
\Theta(T^{-n}\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})
,

\mathcal{N}_{\ell_{B}}(w)\leq CT\ell_{B}^{-3/n}N^{\alpha/n}\leq CTN^{\epsilon}.

if
n
is large enough.
Decomposing balls of radius
k\ell_{B}
into balls of radius
\ell_{B}
, we find

\mathcal{N}_{k\ell_{B}}(w)\leq CTk^{3}N^{\epsilon}.

if
n
is large enough. Similarly, we can show (
93
). In fact,

\begin{split}&\lVert\mathcal{N}_{k\ell_{B}}^{1/2}(w)\prod_{j=1}^{m}a_{x_{j}}\Theta(T^{-n}\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})\zeta\rVert^{2}\\
&\leq\lVert\mathcal{N}_{k\ell_{B}}^{1/2}(w)\Theta\Big(T^{-n}\int dy\,\big(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(y)+1+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\chi_{\ell_{B}}(y-x_{m})\big)^{n}/N^{\alpha}\Big)\,\prod_{j=1}^{m}a_{x_{j}}\zeta\rVert^{2}\\
&\leq CTk^{3}N^{\epsilon}\lVert\prod_{j=1}^{m}a_{x_{j}}\Theta(T^{-n}\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})\zeta\rVert^{2}.\end{split}

(98)

To prove (
94
), we set
\xi=\exp(A^{*}\Theta-\Theta A)\Omega
and we observe that, splitting
\check{A}
as we did in Lemma
8
,

\begin{split}&\lvert\langle\xi,[\mathcal{L}_{n},\int dx_{1}dx_{2}dx_{3}\check{A}(x_{1}-x_{2},x_{1}-x_{3})a_{x_{1}}^{*}a_{x_{2}}^{*}a_{x_{3}}^{*}\Theta(\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})]\xi\rangle\rvert\\
&\leq\int dx_{1}dx_{2}dx_{3}dw\,|\check{A}(x_{1}-x_{2},x_{1}-x_{3})|\,\lvert\langle\xi,[(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+1)^{n},a_{x_{1}}^{*}a_{x_{2}}^{*}a_{x_{3}}^{*}]\Theta(\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})\xi\rangle\rvert\\
&\leq\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\int dx_{1}dx_{2}dx_{3}dw\,|\check{A}(x_{1}-x_{2},x_{1}-x_{3})|\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt\times\big|\langle\xi,(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+1)^{k}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\chi_{\ell_{B}}(x_{i}-w)a_{x_{1}}^{*}a_{x_{2}}^{*}a_{x_{3}}^{*}(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+1)^{n-k-1}\Theta(\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})\xi\rangle\big|\\
&\leq C\sum_{u,v\in\Lambda_{B}}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\int dx_{1}dx_{2}dx_{3}dw\lvert\check{A}_{u,v}(x_{1}-x_{2},x_{1}-x_{3})\rvert\chi_{\ell_{B}}(x_{i}-w)\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt\times\lVert a_{x_{1}}a_{x_{2}}a_{x_{3}}\Theta(4^{-n}\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+1)^{(n-1)/2}\xi\rVert\lVert(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+1)^{(n-1)/2}\xi\rVert.\\
\end{split}

Here we used the fact that
\Theta
is monotonically decreasing

\displaystyle\Theta(\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})\leq\Theta\Big(4^{-n}\int dw\,(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\chi_{\ell_{B}}(x_{i}-w)+1)^{n}/N^{\alpha}\Big)

and the pull-through formula

\displaystyle\int dw\,(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\chi_{\ell_{B}}(x_{i}-w)+1)^{n}a_{x_{1}}a_{x_{2}}a_{x_{3}}=a_{x_{1}}a_{x_{2}}a_{x_{3}}\mathcal{L}_{n}.

Cauchy-Schwarz yields

\begin{split}&\lvert\langle\xi,[\mathcal{L}_{n},\int dxdydz\check{A}(x_{1}-x_{2},x_{1}-x_{3})a_{x_{1}}^{*}a_{x_{2}}^{*}a_{x_{3}}^{*}\Theta(\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})]\xi\rangle\rvert\\
&\leq C\sum_{u,v\in\Lambda_{B}}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Big(\int dx_{1}dx_{2}dx_{3}dw\,\chi_{\ell_{B}}(x_{i}-w)\mathbbm{1}(\lvert x_{1}-x_{2}-u\rvert,\lvert x_{1}-x_{3}-v\rvert\leq\ell_{B})\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt\times\lVert a_{x_{1}}a_{x_{2}}a_{x_{3}}\Theta(4^{-n}\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+1)^{\frac{(n-1)}{2}}\xi\rVert^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\\
&\hskip 8.5359pt\times\Big(\int dx_{1}dx_{2}dx_{3}dw\lvert\check{A}_{u,v}(x_{1}-x_{2},x_{1}-x_{3})\rvert^{2}\chi_{\ell_{B}}(x_{i}-w)\lVert(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+1)^{\frac{(n-1)}{2}}\xi\rVert^{2}\Big)^{1/2}.\end{split}

Consider, for example, the term associated with
i=1
(the other terms can be handled similarly). For every
\alpha,\epsilon>0
we can bound this contribution by

\begin{split}C&\sum_{u,v\in\Lambda_{B}}\lVert\check{A}_{u,v}\rVert_{2}\Big(\int dx_{1}dw\chi_{\ell_{B}}(x_{1}-w)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\lVert\mathcal{N}_{\ell_{B}}^{1/2}(x_{1}-u)\mathcal{N}_{6\ell_{B}}^{1/2}(x_{1}-v)a_{x_{1}}\Theta(4^{-n}\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+1)^{\frac{(n-1)}{2}}\xi\rVert^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\times\Big(\int dx_{1}dw\,\chi_{\ell_{B}}(x_{1}-w)\lVert(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+1)^{\frac{(n-1)}{2}}\xi\rVert^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\\
&\leq CN^{\epsilon}\ell_{B}^{3/2}\sum_{u,v\in\Lambda_{B}}\lVert\check{A}_{u,v}\rVert_{2}\Big(\int dw\,\lVert(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+1)^{\frac{n}{2}}\xi\rVert^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\times\Big(\int dw\,\lVert(\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)+1)^{\frac{(n-1)}{2}}\xi\rVert^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\\
&\leq CN^{-\beta/8+\epsilon}\langle\xi,\mathcal{L}_{n}\xi\rangle\end{split}

(99)

if
n
is large enough. Here, we used (
93
) twice, to control
\mathcal{N}_{\ell_{B}}^{1/2}(x_{1}-u)
and
\mathcal{N}_{\ell_{B}}^{1/2}(x_{1}-v)
, and we applied Lemma
8
.
Choosing
0<\epsilon<\beta/8
, we conclude that

\Big|\frac{d}{dt}\langle e^{t(A^{*}\Theta-\Theta A)}\Omega,\mathcal{L}_{n}e^{t(A^{*}\Theta-\Theta A)}\Omega\rangle\Big|\leq C\langle e^{t(A^{*}\Theta-\Theta A)}\Omega,\mathcal{L}_{n}e^{t(A^{*}\Theta-\Theta A)}\Omega\rangle

for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
Applying Gronwall’s lemma in the interval
t\in[0;1]
, we obtain (
94
). Eq. (
95
) is an immediate consequence.

To show (
96
), we first observe that

\mathcal{N}\leq C\ell_{B}^{-3}\int dw\,\mathcal{N}_{\chi_{\ell_{B}}}(w)\leq C\ell_{B}^{-3}\mathcal{L}^{1/n}_{n}.

Therefore, on the support of the cutoff
\Theta
, we have

\mathcal{N}^{m}\leq C\ell_{B}^{-3m}N^{m\varepsilon}\leq CN^{m(3\kappa/2-\beta/4+\epsilon)}

(100)

for any
\varepsilon>0
, if
n
is large enough (depending on
\alpha,\epsilon
). Setting
\zeta_{s}=\exp(s(A^{*}\Theta-\Theta A))\zeta
, we obtain

\begin{split}&\Big|\langle\exp(A^{*}\Theta-\text{h.c.}\ )\zeta,\mathcal{N}^{m}\exp(A^{*}\Theta-\text{h.c.}\ )\Omega\rangle-\langle\xi,\mathcal{N}^{m}\zeta\rangle\Big|\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{1}ds\,\Big|\langle\zeta_{s},\big[\mathcal{N}^{m},A^{*}\big]\Theta(\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})\zeta_{s}\rangle\Big|\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{1}ds\,\int dx_{1}dx_{2}dx_{3}\,\big|\check{A}(x_{1}-x_{2},x_{1}-x_{3})\big|\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\times\|a_{x_{1}}a_{x_{2}}a_{x_{3}}\Theta(\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})(\mathcal{N}+1)^{(m-1)/2}\zeta_{s}\|\big|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{(m-1)/2}\Theta(\mathcal{L}_{n}/N^{\alpha})\zeta_{s}\|\\
